# Implementation decisions and assumptions

Decisions and working assumptions made while implementing
[`implementation-plan.md`](implementation-plan.md), recorded for later review.
Each entry says where it came from and when to revisit it. Entries marked
**assumed** were made on the maintainer's behalf without a direct answer.

## Decisions (maintainer answers, 2026-10-21)

| ID | Decision | Consequence in the plan | Revisit when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-1 | Proxy nodes must be supported | The client uses signed-URL media mode: it sends neither `header_authenticated_media_v1` nor `authorized_media_origins_v1`. Proxy-origin plan URLs carry their own signed path token and get no account headers; API-origin media and subtitle URLs get `Authorization` and the profile headers. `authorized_media_origins_v1` was not chosen because it sends the account's `Authorization` header to proxy origins, which AGENTS.md forbids ("never to a proxy or other origin named in a playback plan"). New risk: signed HLS playlists for long titles use `#EXT-X-DEFINE` (HLS v8), so test H2 becomes a Milestone 0a gate and server issue S-21 is drafted | If H2 fails, or if the maintainers decide to relax the AGENTS.md rule in favour of header-authenticated proxies |
| D-2 | Bound resolution by the decoder, not the output mode, where possible | Plan section 6.1 unchanged | H4 results |
| D-3 | Dolby Vision through an HLS copy remux with device-side DV evidence | Plan sections 6.1–6.2 unchanged | H3 results |
| D-4 | Deep links use the most recent non-child profile and skip PIN-locked profiles until Roku rules on PIN prompts | Plan section 7 unchanged | Roku's answer |
| D-5 | The server provides trickplay | The scrubber relies on server sheets; a file without sheets shows time only. Roku's answer on files without previews is still requested but is no longer a public-release prerequisite | Roku's answer, or certification feedback |
| D-6 | Devices without multichannel AAC get stereo AAC, with a stop-and-restart when a plan returns multichannel AAC | Plan section 6.1 unchanged; against a server with `codec_channel_ceilings_v1` the per-codec ceiling replaces it (AG-320) | H9 showing a downmix |
| D-7 | (2026-10-25) Release builds match development builds: the hardware gates (plan section 6.2, G1–G14) are removed, a server without `request_origin` is accepted with a warning in every build (a mismatch is still refused), and the `silocontrol` and `handoff` switches are on in every build | `source/playback/gates.bs` is gone; every format claim follows the device's probe (spec section 9.2), except DTS, which waits for S-8; one example of each claim was played on the Ultra 4850X in hardware run 30. Credential confinement on old servers now rests on the server not redirecting (A-14, A-20); handoff ships before Roku confirms it. The `companion` switch was already on in every build, by the owner's earlier decision for companion sign-in (spec section 7.2, `companion-pairing.md` section 1) | Roku's answer on handoff and the receiver; a format that fails on a device whose probe passes it |
| D-8 | (2026-10-26) The feature list after comparing Roku with tvOS and Android TV (silo-apple `2c8f2e1b`, silo-android `9867edfa4`): Skip Credits with `playback.auto_skip_credits` joins the first release; spec section 3.3 lists what is planned after it (Watchlist, Favorites, and History; person pages; trailers and extras; filters and Shuffle; Requests, Calendar, and For You; chapters, speed, aspect, and a sleep timer; spoiler hiding; theme music; the buffering-quality pill; the AI-translation marker; subtitle search and download; multiple saved servers; profile creation and editing) and what is undecided; music and audiobooks are out; a notifications inbox and collection creation and editing leave the list because no Silo TV app offers them | Spec sections 3.2–3.4, 5.7, 5.9, and 15 | The owner orders the planned list, or decides an undecided item |

## Assumptions

| ID | Assumption | Reason | Revisit when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A-1 | **Assumed.** Server issues in plan section 11 stay drafts; nothing is filed on GitHub | Filing is public text the maintainers have not authorized | The maintainers ask for them to be filed |
| A-2 | **Assumed.** Work is committed on local branches only; nothing is pushed and no PR is opened. The integration branch is the plan's worktree branch; each step is a reviewable commit | Pushing and opening PRs are outward-facing and were not explicitly requested | The maintainers ask for pushes or PRs |
| A-3 | **Assumed.** Open PR #3 (`feat/roku-remote-control`, `scripts/roku.sh`) is merged into the integration branch so agents can drive the dev Roku | It touches different files and gives ECP keypresses and screenshots | PR #3 changes or is closed |
| A-4 | **Assumed.** `LICENSE` is AGPL-3.0-or-later, copied from silo-server, and the trademark notice follows the other first-party Silo repositories | The README promises both before implementation starts | Maintainer review |
| A-5 | **Assumed.** Device runs use the dev Roku configured for the remote-control work (through its relay), one agent at a time; sideloading replaces whatever dev app is installed | It is the only configured device | A second device or a conflict with other work |
| A-6 | **Assumed.** Code that can be tested headless is built first; hardware gates run when a device and a reachable dev Silo server with media are available, and Milestone 0a is not reported as exited until they pass | Hardware and a reachable server are not yet confirmed | A device-reachable dev server exists |
| A-7 | **Assumed.** Owner items in plan section 9.1 (Roku account, signing key, reviewer server, `sca-cmd` mirror) are the maintainers'; until a mirror is chosen, CI downloads `sca-cmd` from Roku's URL, checks the pinned sha256, and caches it | They need people, accounts, or money | The maintainers assign owners |
| A-8 | **Assumed.** Tool versions follow plan section 8; when a pinned version is not on npm, the nearest available release is used and recorded here | Versions were checked once, on 2026-10-21 | Each dependency bump |
| A-9 | **Assumed.** Claude Opus agents implement; a Codex agent reviews each merged wave, and findings are fixed before the next wave | Independent review on a different model caught the most plan errors | Process review |
| A-10 | **Assumed.** Device runs proceed when the other device on the TV's HDMI 1 input reports `Idle` (a read-only `playing` query); then `key Home` returns the TV to Roku Home. The dev device is a TCL 55R625 Roku TV (model A109X, Roku OS 15.3.4, 4K, Dolby Vision panel) in shared use. Device runs happen only when the TV shows the Roku home screen or the dev app; agents never switch away from an HDMI input or another app in use. It covers the Roku TV and 4K DV cohorts only; the 512 MB, non-OpenGL, reference, and no-DV cohorts need other devices | At first check the TV was on another HDMI input | More devices arrive |
| A-11 | **Assumed.** Hardware tests run against an isolated Silo stack on a lab host (with its own throwaway admin account and fixture media), reached from the Roku through the header probe on a LAN host, rather than the shared dev server, which would need an account created there. Proxy-origin tests (D-1) need a proxy reachable from the Roku and wait until one exists | Shared dev or a LAN-reachable stack with a proxy pool becomes available |
| A-12 | **Assumed.** The first hardware run (`docs/hardware-evidence.md`, 2026-10-22) failed H1, H2, and H19 for platform and server reasons, not client architecture. Milestone 1 work starts in parallel while the fixes are prototyped; Milestone 0a is still not reported as exited until those gates pass | Plan section 9.2 says Milestone 0a gates Milestone 1 |
| A-13 | **Assumed.** Server fixes (S-15 TS copy packaging, S-20 redirect guarantees, S-21/S-22 playlist shape) are prototyped on a local silo-server branch, deployed only to the isolated lab sandbox, and never pushed; they become proposals for the server maintainers | The maintainers take them up |
| A-14 | **Assumed.** Roku follows HTTP redirects with no app control and forwards `X-Profile-Id`/`X-Profile-Token` (and the login body on a 307) to the new origin, and the `Video` node sends all three headers to absolute cross-origin child URIs (H1). The client cannot prevent this, so credential confinement rests on the server: authenticated `/api/v2` and media routes never redirect off-origin and playlists keep relative child URIs (S-20), plus a client setup check that refuses an origin whose `system/info` shows it was redirected, once the server reports its canonical origin. G1 stays development-only until that lands, and the README will tell operators not to redirect `/api/v2` off-origin | S-20 implemented and H1 rerun |
| A-15 | **Assumed.** Embedded text subtitles on originals and remuxes stay available with a "Loading subtitles…" state even though first extraction took about 65 s (H14 target 15 s); later loads are cached. This relaxes plan section 6.8, which would hide them until S-18 | Maintainer review; S-18 |
| A-16 | **Assumed.** H2b showed Roku refuses an HLS media playlist above 4,194,304 bytes (4 MiB), silently truncates a chunked or gzip one there, and stops refreshing an EVENT playlist once it passes 4 MiB, while entry count does not matter and `#EXT-X-DEFINE` works. So the server-side requirement becomes "keep every playlist served to Roku under 4 MiB" (`#EXT-X-DEFINE` compaction of long signed URLs, complete keyframe playlists for copy remuxes), tracked as S-22; the `#EXT-X-DEFINE` opt-out (S-21) is dropped | The R11 refusal is re-measured at its real byte size |
| A-17 | **Assumed.** On the dev TV neither subset Inter nor Roku's system font renders CJK or Arabic (both show boxes), and the system font also misses Vietnamese letters Inter has, so all text stays in Inter and titles in other scripts render as boxes in the first release (a spec 5.9 deviation). Bundling a CJK font does not fit the 4 MB package; a downloadable font is a later decision. `monospacedDigits` renders boxes with Inter, so timecodes use an Inter variant whose default digits are tabular | A font strategy for other scripts |
| A-18 | **Assumed.** Step M0-8 (plain diagnostic sign-in, profile, and PIN screens) is skipped: the dev harness already exercises on-device sign-in, and the production setup, sign-in, profile, and PIN screens (M1-4, M1-5) build directly on M0-7's session API after navigation (M1-2) | Plan section 9.3 lists M0-8 |
| A-19 | **Assumed.** Run 2 showed the prototype's MPEG-TS copy remux makes the Roku report real audio for every codec pair (H19, audibility not checked remotely), so S-15 is the chosen fix and the client sends `hls_copy_mpegts_v1` whenever the server advertises it. The same run showed complete keyframe playlists for long 4K titles exceed 4 MiB (up to 6.3 MB) and are refused, so S-22 (`#EXT-X-DEFINE` compaction or a byte cap for playlists served to Roku) is now blocking; Dolby Vision engagement (H3) is still untested because the harness does not claim DV | S-22 prototype and a DV-claiming run |
| A-20 | (Superseded by [D-7](#decisions-maintainer-answers-2026-10-21) and S-20: every build accepts a server without `request_origin`, with a warning, and refuses a mismatch; the server reports the field since S-20, silo-server #3023, merged 2026-10-25.) **Assumed.** Review W45-01: because Roku follows redirects invisibly, release builds refuse to send any credential unless `GET /api/v2/system/info` reports a `request_origin` equal to the configured origin (checked at setup and on every launch); debug builds proceed with a warning when the field is absent but still refuse a mismatch. A stock Silo server has no `request_origin`, so a release build cannot sign in until the server adds it (prototyped as S-20 on `roku/client-support`) | The server maintainers adopt S-20 |

| AG-107 | Server prototype 5 | `roku/client-support` at `63ecc659f`. Run 5's 7–9 s backward reanchors were the lab's shared Postgres stalling on writes (progress POSTs took 1–7 s and a checkpoint spent 6 s in fsync in that window), not the seek direction; on a quiet host both directions answer in 0.2–0.5 s. Rapid seeks no longer wait up to 5 s for the replaced transcode job to stop under the session lock (`47b241582`), and each replan logs its phase timings. The 4d stall was throughput (52.8 Mb/s delivered against a 55.5 Mb/s stream), and the client's `quality_bandwidth_limit` replan was the right response. Hardware-run timings depend on the lab host's disk load. | AG-110 | Player fixes / W7-4 | Replay's caption span is pending until its merged seek commits, and only then forces captions (`replayCaptions` active), when the commit's target is below where Replay was pressed. Back before the commit drops a pending span with nothing to restore; a span an earlier Replay committed survives that Back and ends where it was pressed. The first playback report after the commit decides whether the seek landed inside the span (the player reports the session's seek target at once, `Player.commitSeek` then `refreshChrome`); a report at or past the span's end (the session dropped the seek, or merged skips went past it) ends the span and restores "Instant replay". Any later report at or past the end, a seek from Skip Intro included, ends it, as does `hide` (an error screen or Up Next covering the player). The span's end can still come early if Roku reports a position from before a local seek after it, as before | A device run of Replay in "Instant replay" mode, with Back during the 200 ms merge |

So when Roku's caption mode becomes anything but Off, from Options' Captions rows or a platform event that is not the echo of Silo's own write, while the plan selects no subtitle and the playing content carries subtitle tracks (`content.subtitleTracks`, a late-added selected one included), and after an in-place plan finds that state, the player sends a `track_change` with the plan's selection (`inPlace` false); its plan reloads into content that `playback.sidecars.offered` gives no sidecars while captions are on. On replay and On mute count as On for a burned-in subtitle (it shows throughout; the pixels cannot appear only during a replay or while muted), and Options offers only Off and On, with On checked for any mode but Off, while a subtitle is burned in or set aside. A plan that arrives burned in while captions are Off (a Detail pick, or a replan) is asked once per caption mode, so a server that burns it in again is not asked in a loop. | AG-114 | W8 fixes / W8-2 (revises AG-109) | Same media for an in-place subtitle switch also needs the same file named in both plans: `effective_media_file_id` and `source.media_file_id` present and equal (compared as text, so the opaque string IDs and the schema's integers both work), and equal `effective_recipe` (present), `transformations`, and `selected_tracks.audio` (`id` and `index`; absent in both for a file without audio), beside AG-109's delivery, stream protocol, container, and URL without `st`, timeline offset and origin, and client-rendered subtitle modes. | AG-137 | Lifecycle fixes / run 8 defect 5 (revises AG-37) | A failed refresh's backoff (`notBefore`) now ends early when it began without an answer from the server: the refresh got no response, or a `502`, `503`, or `504` without `Retry-After` (a gateway's answer for a stopped upstream, as the lab's probe and a reverse proxy give). The next answer from the configured origin with a status from 200 to 499, whatever its auth (Retry's `system/info`, any read, or the `401` being handled), ends it, so that `401` refreshes at once instead of answering `auth_unavailable`. | AG-138 | Lifecycle fixes / run 8 defect 4 (extends AG-79) | The session reducer counts the server unreachable after a start, replan, or progress post that got no answer or a `502`, `503`, or `504` (`playback.player.answerEvent` now carries the status). A `500` and every other answer still count as reachable. So a start that a proxy answers `502` six times ends with Retry and Try Anyway, and that Retry checks `system/info` first; a replan answered with a gateway status after the first frame joins the reconnect, as protocol section 6.2 lists a 5xx among the answers that wait for the next try. | AG-139 | Lifecycle fixes / run 8 defect 3 (T2c) | Cause: after the T2a restart the replan for the lost session answered `404`, the reducer started a new attempt without stopping the old session (the server said it was gone), but `playback.worker` still tracked that session's start; when the player was released it sent the "its player left" stop with no sample, and the server rebuilt the session from its Postgres attempt row and upserted its last progress (413.988 s) over the new session's stop (468.584 s). Fix: the worker forgets a start's session when a replan or progress post answers `notFound` or `ended`, and when the same player (owner) sends a newer attempt's start, since its reducer has by then handed over or given up every earlier session. Also, fences now name the title (`contentId`, the player's item ID): a job with a position retires the queued jobs of the title's older sessions, an accepted progress post does the same, and a job waits while another session's job for its title is in flight. Each title remembers its newest session with a serial, so an older job that was in flight when a newer position was queued or accepted is retired when it comes back for a retry, and an answer to a progress post sent before a newer session's position retires nothing. Not covered: an older job in flight that the server accepts after a newer session's progress post (only the server can order those). **For the server maintainers:** a stop for a session the server no longer holds, especially one without a final sample, should not move the resume point backwards: either refuse it (`404`, as v1 does) or write it with `SetProgressIfNewer` against the newest attempt for the item, since `RecordPlaybackStop` and `UpdateProgress` are last-write-wins across sessions | Device run: T2a then T2c again, expecting no second `DELETE` and a Resume at the new session's stop minus 7 s |
| AG-140 | Lifecycle fixes / run 8 defects 1–2 (extends AG-47 and AG-114) | Media credentials. From the server (read at silo-server `origin/main` `db95c13bd` and `roku/client-support`, not run): API-origin media routes are `ClassProfileScoped` behind `RequireAuth`, which reads `Authorization` before anything else and answers `401` for an expired token; `st` (24 h, `MaxTokenTTL`) is never read while the session lives; plans' `expires_at` and proxy path tokens are also a fixed 24 h; so on the configured origin the media credential's lifetime is the access token's (`auth.access_token_expiry`, default `8h`). Classification: Roku's error −5 (`mediaerror`) quotes the segment's status as `last_err:NNN`/`lasterror:NNN` in `errorStr` (and possibly `errorInfo.dbgmsg`); `playback.video` reads it before the error code, so 401/403 is `auth`, 404/410 `ended` (configured origin), other 5xx `server`. Renewal (spec 9.4): `mediaHeaders` now reports the token's `expiresAt` and `refreshAt`; the reducer renews from `refreshAt` at the next local seek or in-place track change, `CREDENTIAL_LEAD_SECONDS` (60) before `expiresAt` wherever playback is (not while paused or seeking, or with a replan in flight), and on `auth` failures, a stall after expiry, or buffering after expiry. A renewal is a reload with `refresh` (mediaHeaders forces a refresh while the token is the generation that failed) or, for a copy remux or a proxy URL, a `seek_reanchor` at the position with no failure; it never sets the plan's transport reopen or records `transport_reopen`. A renewal is not repeated for headers that bring no later expiry (`renewedExpiry`, per plan and carried on by a renewing reanchor's plan); renewed headers refused again before they play show the error (`media_unauthorized`). Interaction with AG-114: every renewal reload plays the current plan's own URL, so it also renews a kept `st`; AG-114's own renewal (3600 s before a kept URL's `expires_at`) reloads with headers that mediaHeaders refreshes when inside the refresh window. Proxy URLs renew from `expires_at` − 3600 s. With the lab's 3-minute tokens a playing title reloads about every 90–120 s; with 8-hour tokens at most once per token. A media `404` while a replan is in flight waits for that replan's answer (the replan retires the transport). Headless only | Device run: T1a and T1b again with 3-minute tokens (expect "renewing the media credential before it expires" about a minute before each expiry, no segment 401, no `decoder_failure` or `decoder_no_output`); a long pause across an expiry; a proxy-node lab |
| AG-141 | Lifecycle fixes / run 8 defect 1 (outage; refines AG-140) | After the first frame, a media `5xx` reconnects at once (no local reopen, which would ask the failing server again), and so does a network error, a stall, or a `no_output` stall while the API is also unreachable (a gateway's `5xx` counts, AG-138): Roku's buffering-status changes count as input, so run 8 T3c's outage stall was classed `decoder_no_output`. A network error or stall while the API answers keeps plan 6.7's one reopen, then `failure_recovery` (the route failed). Before the first frame a `5xx` is `failure_recovery` `http_failure` (`sourceRefused` for a proxy). Run 7's "refused connection" path (`status 0`) is unchanged and still unexercised (run 8 defect 7) | Device run: T2a and T3a again, expecting `track_change` instead of `failure_recovery` and "Reconnecting" during the outage |
| AG-142 | Lifecycle fixes / run 8 defect 6 | Cause not confirmed. What the evidence shows (console, ECP, and probe logs of run 8): the client sent `start_position` = saved − 7 s and the server's target matched; the server sets `player_start_seconds` = requested − keyframe origin (`configureCopyRemuxTimelineV3`), about 1.5 s for T4a; the player sets `PlayStart` to it, and nothing seeks before the first frame. Yet three of run 8's four copy-remux resumes started late: T2a at 7.17 s (`player_start_seconds` ≈ 1.12), T3 pass 1 at 9.42 s (≈ 0.46), and T4a at 8.04 s (≈ 1.54), each on a playlist still growing at its first fetch (ECP duration short of the remaining runtime). The fourth, whose playlist was complete at once, started at 0.0 s, its segment's start, before the target. So on Roku OS 15.3.4 a growing remux playlist does not start at `PlayStart`; why Roku picks a later segment is unknown. Change: the plan log now carries `player_start_seconds`; an HLS load whose first positions run more than 3 s past its start (less the time played) within 3 s of playing is sought back to that start once per load, with a `[WARN] player: the load started past its start; seeking back` line (`start`, `position`); a viewer's seek in that window ends the check. That seek is local even on a copy remux: it stays inside the load's own playlist, before what has played. If Roku also lands the seek late, the log shows it and the next step is the server's (start the remux's playlist complete, or `#EXT-X-START`) | Device run: Resume a library episode on a copy remux three times, checking ECP's first playing position against the plan log's `player_start_seconds`, and whether the seek back lands |
| AG-143 | W9 review / W9-1 (refines AG-114 and AG-140) | A media failure (any kind: `auth`, `ended`, `server`, network, stall, decode, …) that arrives while a replan is in flight is held on the plan (`plan.failure`) and handled when that replan settles, instead of queuing a recovery a newer plan would fence away or reloading at once. An answer that adopts a new load replaces the failed content and drops the failure; `canRetrack` refuses a failed load (or one with a renewal owed), so an in-place `track_change` answer for failed content is adopted as a new load at the plan's `player_start_seconds` (the position the replan named, not the failure's later position). An answer that leaves the failed content (a refused intent) runs the failure's own recovery as if it had just arrived, so a media `404` after a refused change restarts at the position without a stop, as a `404` with nothing in flight does; that the server answered the replan does not override the media's `404`. A viewer seek dispatched onto failed content moves the position the recovery uses and sends no `seek` to the Video node. A renewal is owed (`state.renewal`, the expiry it renews) from an `auth` failure during a replan, or a renewal sent as a `seek_reanchor`, until a load with refreshed headers is scheduled: a reload, or any adopted plan's load (including a `seek_failure_recovery` after the reanchor is refused, and the next attempt after a `404`); only then are `renewLoad` and `renewedExpiry` recorded. The trade-off: a local reopen or renewal no longer runs during a replan that is waiting to be retried, so failed content can wait for a retry backoff (at most the replan's retry budget, or the two-minute reconnect) before it recovers. Player: a header answer for a load the reducer has since replaced is ignored (`isCurrent`), and a stale item the slot held for a Video stop never plays (`playItem`); both now have probe coverage. Headless only | Device run with 3-minute tokens: change subtitles in place on a copy remux just before a token expiry (expect one `refresh` load, no retrack of the failed content, no second segment `401`); stop the API container during a quality change, then start it (expect the quality answer's load or the deferred recovery, never a frozen error frame) |
| AG-144 | W9 review / W9-2 (refines AG-139) | `playback.worker.addJob` releases a job whose fence (server, account, profile) differs from the identity whose credentials ApiTask holds before anything else: no queue admission, no `forgetSession`, no title note or supersession; it logs `playback job identity_changed` and answers `fenced` (the player does not read job answers). Stops the worker builds itself (`stopStart`) pass the same gate. A progress post's accepted answer supersedes only while its fence is the current identity's. `playback.jobs` keys titles by `titleOf(fence)`, the JSON array of server, account, profile, and `contentId`, for supersession on add and on accepted progress, `finish`'s newer-session check, and in-flight holds, so the same content under another identity is another title. During a deferred identity change (the 10-second settle of spec section 9.5) ApiTask still holds the leaving identity, so its late jobs are admitted and sent as before. The reproduction is headless only; the device/UI ordering that would hand over an old profile's stop after the new profile's is not established (render-thread teardown usually delivers it first) | Device run: switch profiles mid-title, resume the same title under the new profile, stop it while the API container is stopped behind the lab probe, then start it again (expect the new profile's stop to land with its final position, and no `superseded` for it) |
| AG-145 | Device run 9 (coordinator decisions) | Run 9 (`f1d9d9b`, lab access tokens of 3 minutes) proved proactive media credential renewal on copy remuxes (7 renewals, no 401 in 2,945 probe lines, no `failure_recovery`), renewal after a 4-minute pause and after a seek past expiry, recovery from a lab API restart without the stale stop (run 8 T2c fixed), Try Anyway kept during a 5xx outage, immediate Retry after the server returned (3.2 s to first frame), the parity fixes it could exercise (scrub clocks, partial rewatch, both finale cases, Play pill widths), Back during start, a rapid Up Next chain, profile and server changes during a pending stop, and a stable node count. Defects: a renewal reload on an original counted as a bandwidth stall and stepped Auto down to a transcode; Play committing a seek after expiry stayed paused; copy-remux loads on a growing playlist start about 8 s late and the client's seek back never lands (Resume loses its rewind; Play from the start skips 8 s); a viewer skip in that window is lost; a start replaced by a newer attempt was replayed and stopped after the player left. Decisions: (1) the 8 s late start is pursued on the server first (a complete playlist at start or `#EXT-X-START`, tried in the lab prototype and on the TV), keeping the client seek-back only if it is proven to land; (2) a start given up for a newer attempt is settled without a stop, as spec 9.5 says; (3) the lab keeps 3-minute tokens until the next run verifies the renewal fixes, then returns to 1 hour; (4) the run switched the dev app from the owner's server to the lab, which ended only the local session | Run 9 fix round; server prototype 8; device run 10 |
| AG-146 | Run 9 fixes / run 9 defect 1 (refines AG-140) | Cause: `playback.session.onPlayer` classed buffering as `reload` only before a plan's first frame, and Auto quality's ten-second grace ran only from a plan's first frame, so a renewal's local reload of an original (4.5 s, then 2.1 s of rebuffering 90 s apart in T1b) counted as two stalls in two minutes and stepped down to a transcode. Fix: buffering before the current load's first frame is `reload` for any load (a plan's start, a reopen, a credential renewal, a kept URL's renewal at a seek), and a later load's first frame restarts the grace (`playback.autoQuality.loadStarted`) without clearing the plan's stall history or its bitrate, and not while a step down waits for its plan. A stall past the grace still counts, with the stalls before the reload. | AG-147 | Run 9 fixes / run 9 defect 2 | Cause: Play committing a ladder seek while paused gave the controls' `seek` command and then `togglePause`; once the credential had passed its refresh time the seek became a renewal's local reload whose `reopen` took `autoplay` from `state.player.paused` (still true), and `togglePause` then resumed the old content, which the new load replaced paused (T1e). Fix: the controls' `seek` command carries `resume` (Play committed it while paused); the player passes it in `seekTo`, and the session reducer counts the viewer as playing before it decides, so a renewal's reload, a kept URL's reload, or a reanchor opens playing; OK's commit (`resume` false) keeps the pause. `togglePause` leaves alone content that a newer load is about to replace (`m.current.load` differs from `m.activeLoad`), so the old position never plays before the reload; a Pause pressed in that window is not applied to the new load either (as before the change, when the reload's `autoplay` was already decided) | Device run with 3-minute tokens: T1d and T1e again (pause past expiry; Rev Rev then OK stays paused at the target; Rev Rev then Play plays from the target with no glimpse of the old position) |
| AG-148 | Run 9 fixes / run 9 defects 3–4 (refines AG-142 and AG-78) | T4b's lost skip was not dropped by the session reducer: the console shows one `controls shown` at the start and none at either Right, so the controls the start showed were still up (kept while starting and buffering, then 5 s) and Right moved focus from Play/Pause to Skip Forward, Android TV's rule for shown controls; the seek back made that window longer (its `seeking` and Roku's 1.5–5 s rebuffer kept the controls busy). Changes: (1) the controls the start shows hide at the content's first frame unless a key came since, it opens paused, or a scrub or panel keeps them (tvOS hides them at the first frame), so Left and Right skip from then on; before the first frame they keep Android TV's focus rule, and a seek during the start is still not sent (a spec 5.9 row records the deviation; superseded: the start now shows no controls and takes only Back until the first frame, spec 5.7); (2) the seek back stays one attempt per load but is not the viewer's: it no longer sets `player.seeking` or moves `player.position` (progress keeps what the node reports), and a viewer's seek, sent or queued, disarms the check and replaces it (`requestSeek`); (3) the first position reading after the node plays again (or 3 s on) logs `the seek back landed` (info) or `the seek back did not land` (warn) with `start` and `position`. | AG-149 | Run 9 fixes / run 9 defect 5 (refines AG-139) | Cause: `playback.worker.trackStart` forgot a player's earlier starts on a newer attempt only when they had a session, so Try Anyway's start, answered `502` six times and given up by the reducer, stayed tracked without one; when the player left after Retry's session, `cancel` marked it orphaned, replayed it (`201`) and stopped the session it created with no sample (T3d). Fix: a player's newer attempt forgets every earlier start of that player, answered or not, in flight or not: none is replayed or stopped. Reconciling plan 6.6: "replayed verbatim until certain" is the reducer's rule while the start is the attempt it plays, and it ends there in an answer or in the error after five replays; "a newer user intent waits for an in-flight mutation to settle, then supersedes it" is what happened here (Retry came after that error settled the start), so the superseded start needs no outcome. If it did reach the server, its session gets no stop from the client and the server's inactivity expiry ends it (about 45 s); silo-server's `docs/playback-api.md` says an expired session is stopped under a server-minted `stop_id` (read, not run), so not replaying it writes nothing the expiry would not, and usually nothing at all (a gateway's `502` means the start never reached the server). The worker's own replay stays for a player's newest start whose answer was uncertain when the player left (Back during start), whose position is the newest. Headless only | Device run: T3b–T3d again (Try Anyway while down, Retry once back, Back after 60 s; expect one `DELETE` with a sample and no `start` after it) |

| AG-165 | Run 11 fixes / run 11 defect 1 (AG-162 decision 4; refines AG-159) | Cause: the player sent every `bufferingStatus` and `downloadedSegment` change as `input`, and the stall clock called any stall after input `decoder_no_output`, which the server treats like every `failure_recovery`: it adds the failed plan's key, so the route is excluded (silo-server `origin/main` `playback_v3.go` 5287–5307; no classification but `sourceRefused` is special-cased). Fix: the player sends the fill (`bufferingStatus.percentage`) with each reading (`playback.video.bufferEvent`) and segments as before; `playback.recovery.noteMedia` restarts the clock when media reaches a node waiting for it (before the load plays, or rebuffering) and the buffer still fills (a reading above the last, or a segment below 100%); a full buffer, or media while the node reports playing, marks decoder input. At 20 s without progress the clock gives `no_output` (`decoder_no_output`) after decoder input and `stall` (`transport_stall`: the one reopen, then `failure_recovery`) otherwise, and logs `no progress for 20 s` with the kind and the last fill. A slow start or rebuffer therefore waits as long as its buffer keeps filling, with no upper bound, as Android TV's start does; Auto quality still steps down after a first frame (AG-146 keeps a load's own start out of it), and an explicit quality keeps rebuffering. Android TV restarts on buffer growth only before its decoder has input and reads a frozen decoder from its counters; Roku has none, so a full buffer stands in for decoder input. Assumed, not seen on the device: `bufferingStatus.percentage` is 0–100, as Roku's Video reference documents (run 11's console did not log it; ECP shows the same fill in tenths of a percent); if it were in tenths, a stall that stops between 10% and 100% would still read `decoder_no_output`. Specs replay the three run 11 sequences from the ECP readings. Narrowing, per the server's schema (silo-server `origin/main` `31fabc723`, read, not run): `videoEligibleV3` checks a `video_decode` entry's `profiles` (case-insensitive equality with ffprobe's lowercased profile, "profile 0") under `exact` evidence and its `bit_depths` under every tier, so G11 sends the probe's VP9 entry with only the profiles in its `vp9Profiles` param (`["profile 0"]`) and their bit depths (`[8]`); `codecs_audio` and a delivery's `audio_decode_codecs` are flat lists matched by name, so G12 sends only the probe names in its `codecs` param (`["flac"]`), and Opus and `pcm_s16le`/`pcm_s24le` stay unclaimed though run 11's probe passed them. | AG-175 | Probe lab modes (AG-174 decision 6) | **Assumed.** The header probe gains two modes, off by default, switched while it runs by control files it reads on every request: `hold-replan.PORT` and `refuse-media.PORT` in `--control-dir` (default its working directory), PORT the origin listener's, so the API-origin and proxy-origin probes on the lab host switch separately; a mode is on while its file exists (`scripts/header-probe/README.md`). The hold delays forwarding of `POST /api/v2/playback/{session}/replan` (the file's seconds, 30 when empty, at most 600), so the server receives the replan only after it, which gives run 9's T6c the replan in flight at a profile change; a hold past the client's 15 s request timeout also makes the replan uncertain, and each replay is held again. The refusal resets (or, with `close`, closes) `stream`, `playlist`, and `segment` connections after reading the request, because a per-class refusal cannot happen at connect time; it stands in for run 12's T2b' outage on an original, which the probe answered with 502s. `hold` and `refuse` lines carry status 0 and no secrets. | AG-176 | Spec section 12.5 Audio Guide gaps (AG-173, AG-174 decision 6) | Error screens, status notices, and Options' headings speak through each component's own `roAudioGuide` (`Say(text, flushSpeech, dontRepeat)`), as custom controls already did: Roku reads `audioGuideText` only on a focused `Poster` and in its own lists' content metadata, and reads a `Dialog`'s title, message, then focused button, but gives custom components no speech of their own. Rules: (1) a full-screen error or empty state says its title and message, then its focused action, in one utterance with `flushSpeech` true (it replaces what was being said) and `dontRepeat` false (a Retry that fails the same way is heard again). The player's error and the first-run screens do it through `setControlRows(rows, true, lines)` (`components/views/controls.bs`); MessageScreen on each show; sign-in's providers error before Try again; `StatusView` says its message and second line, with its button only while it has focus, when it takes focus and when its screen calls `announce()` once focus settles (Home, a library, Detail, Detail's episode message; Search already said its own), at most once until it hides, changes, or takes focus again. (2) The status chip (`ControlsOverlay`) says each status it starts to show, with `flushSpeech` false when it appears, so the control the viewer just moved to is heard out, and true when it replaces an earlier status, which would be stale ("Starting…" then "Buffering"); a chip that clears flushes nothing, so a status queued behind other speech can still be heard once just after it clears; the player gives the chip "" while its error shows, so nothing out of sight speaks over the error. (3) Options adds a titled section's heading to the row's speech as focus enters the section ("Captions, Off"), since Roku has no heading role for custom text. Only what the screen shows is said, the server's host included where a message names it; never a URL, token, PIN, or code. The client still never reads `IsAudioGuideEnabled()`; `roAudioGuide` is silent while Audio Guide is off. Headless specs (`tests/views/audioGuide.spec.bs`, `tests/util/speech.spec.bs`) put a fake guide in each component through its probe (`tests/components/fakeAudioGuide.bs`). **Assumed:** Roku speaks these `Say` calls as the reference describes; nothing has been heard on a device. Sources: https://developer.roku.com/docs/references/brightscript/interfaces/ifaudioguide.md, https://developer.roku.com/docs/references/brightscript/components/roaudioguide.md, https://developer.roku.com/dev/docs/text-to-speech | Device pass with Audio Guide on (spec section 12.5): the player's error (unreachable server, then Right to Try Anyway), Buffering and Reconnecting during playback and with Options open, a refused track change, Home or Detail with the network down, a server update or password-off screen, and the Subtitles tab moving down into Captions |
| AG-177 | W10 review / W10-1 (refines AG-146) | Cause: Auto quality's interval for buffering in progress outlived the load it measured. A media 401/403 (or a stall once the credential expired) during buffering renews with a reload, which clears `loadAssigned` and `loadPlayed` but left the interval's `stall` cause running; the run 9 grace restarts only at the reload's first frame, and the reload's own `reload`-cause buffering cannot start before the player assigns it, so six seconds of header refresh, sidecars, or the old node's stop sent a `quality_change` for content never assigned. The same interval could also queue a step-down behind a renewal's `seek_reanchor` and be recorded as a stall when the reload played. Fix: `playback.autoQuality.loadEnded` reclassifies the interval in progress as `reload` (never counted) whenever the current load fails (`handleFailure`, before any deferral) or a reload replaces it (`reload`, which a proactive renewal while buffering also takes), and `runTimers` runs Auto's check only on a load that has played, so nothing is decided between a reload and its first frame; the grace still restarts at that first frame (after assignment), and stalls recorded before the failure stay in the history. Assumed: buffering that ends in a load's failure says nothing about bandwidth, as `noteOutage` already assumes for outages; a genuine bandwidth stall reaches Auto's six-second step-down long before the 20 s stall clock's failure. Specs replay the review's reproduction (buffering, then media `auth`, then 36 s before assignment), a prior stall kept across a renewal, a renewal reanchor on a copy remux, and a proactive renewal due while buffering. Headless only | Device run: a slow renewal during buffering on an original with Auto (expect no `quality_change` before the reload plays, and none for its own start) |
| AG-178 | W10 review / W10-2 (refines AG-165) | Cause: decoder-input evidence outlived the playback it came from. A downloaded segment while the node played set `stall.input`; when media then stopped and the node rebuffered at 0%, nothing cleared it (entering buffering did not, and a 0% reading is no growth), so 20 s later the clock gave `no_output` and the reducer sent `decoder_no_output` at once, skipping the plan's one transport reopen and excluding a route that had been playing (silo-server `playback_v3.go` 5287–5307: every `failure_recovery` adds the failed key). Fix: when the node enters buffering after the load has played, `playback.recovery.rebufferStarted` clears `input` and the last reading but not the clock's mark, so each rebuffer is judged on its own media: a reading or segment that fills restarts the clock as before (AG-165), a full buffer in the wait marks input, and media while the node reports playing still does (the frozen-picture case). A load's first wait already starts clean at assignment (AG-159). Behaviour change: segments without readings during a rebuffer that follows a full reading taken before it no longer count as decoder input. Specs: an empty rebuffer after healthy input takes the reopen, then `failure_recovery` with `transport_stall` and `transport_reopen`; flat and falling readings, growth, a full frozen buffer, and the unreachable-API reconnect. Headless only | Device run: an original whose media is cut mid-play (lab link down, API up) with an explicit quality (expect `no progress for 20 s` with kind `stall`, then the reopen, not `decoder_no_output`) |
| AG-179 | W10 review / W10-3 (refines AG-166) | Cause: `playback.probe.decodeCeiling` gave the VP9 entry the largest size and frame rate the H.264 and HEVC ladders proved, so on a Roku TV whose HEVC answers 5.1 G11 sent VP9 profile 0 at 3840×2160 and 60 fps, though the VP9 query (profile only) says nothing about size or rate and run 11's H7 played a 1080p source; the server's `videoEligibleV3` (silo-server `capabilities_v3.go` 134–159, read at `31fabc723`) trusts those bounds. Fix: the probe's VP9 entry carries only its profiles and bit depths; G11's new `envelope` param (`max_width` 1920, `max_height` 1080, `max_frame_rate` 30) bounds the claim in `playback.gates.vp9Decoder`, intersected with any lower bound the probe's VP9 entry carries (none today), and a debug context's `vp9Envelope` widens it, over the default, for H7 runs of larger shapes. The VP9 claim on the run 11 TV is now `{ codec: "vp9", hardware: true, profiles: ["profile 0"], bit_depths: [8], max_width: 1920, max_height: 1080, max_frame_rate: 30 }`; eight dev-TV goldens change in those three members only. **Assumed:** FX-032 (MKV VP9 profile 0 1080p SDR, AAC 5.1, 2.5 Mbps) is at most 30 fps; run 11 and run 13 did not record its frame rate, so 30 fps is the cap, which turns a 1080p50/60 VP9 original into a transcode. Not changed: VP9 levels are never probed or sent (Roku documents `Level` for VP9 but VP9 sources usually probe an unknown level, which a level bound cannot validate); AV1 still takes `decodeCeiling` while G10 is off on every cohort and must get its own envelope before it opens. G12 borrows nothing: FLAC is a flat name in `codecs_audio` and the delivery audio lists, with no size, rate, or channel bound (multichannel FLAC stays the documented AG-166 limitation). G7's base-layer original is validated against the HEVC ladder's own entries (size, rate, and levels from the HEVC levels that answered), which no other codec widens; a spec pins it with HEVC at level 5.0 beside H.264 at 60 fps. | AG-180 | W10 review / W10-4 (refines AG-154, AG-156, AG-170) | Cause: BrowseRows asks the `RowList` for a restoration's jump on a later callback (`jumpTimer`, 50 ms, up to two asks), and a report on another card left the jump pending, so a viewer's Right in that window was undone: the hero followed the move and then the jump put the ring back on the restored card (and OK after it opened that title). A viewer's move onto one of them inside the window (for example Left back onto the first card) still reads as the list's and keeps the jump. | AG-182 | Device run 14 (coordinator decisions) | Run 14 (`fcf7f2d`) proved the plan 6.6 profile change during an in-flight replan (held 20 s by the probe: the stop went out at once, the switch waited for playback jobs and completed after the replan timed out, the held replan reached the server later and got 404, nothing went out with the new profile's headers), VP9's narrowed claim (the fixture is 1920×1080 at 23.976 fps and played as the original), a viewer move 0.2 s after a return sticking, and a stable node count. It failed on connection resets: refused media gave `-3 … Transfer error` (read as `player`) on a reopen and `-5 … last_err:961` (read as `decode`) on HLS within a second, so each short outage ran `failure_recovery` with `decoder_failure` through every route in 2–4 s and ended on a terminal error with only Back. Home return focus regressed (run 12 defect 1 again, after W10-4's change). Decisions: (1) a media error that names a transport failure (Roku's transfer errors, `last_err` codes that are not HTTP statuses, resets, timeouts) is a network failure: it follows the reconnect path of spec 9.6 with backoff and never excludes a route; only a decoder error with media delivered counts as `decoder_failure`; (2) the Home return regression is fixed before anything else in that area; (3) the probe's refuse mode also cuts connections already open, so an original's single range request can be interrupted; (4) W10-1's renewal-while-buffering case stays headless-only until a lab mode can delay segments rather than refuse them | Run 14 fix round |
| AG-183 | Run 14 fixes / run 14 defect 1 (AG-182 decision 1; supersedes AG-141's reopen for network errors) | Cause: `playback.video.failure` read a connection that failed as `player` (error −3: `reader pick stream error:bad:Transfer error: Failure when receiving data from the peer`, `… Server returned nothing (no headers, no data)`, `… mpr zero length playlist err0`, all category `mediaplayer`) or `decode` (error −5, `mediaerror`: `buffer:loop:demux:Errors seen on all bitrates:last_err:961:…`), and even a `network` kind got one reopen and then `failure_recovery`, which excludes the route while the API answers, so each refused request of a new plan exhausted the routes in 2–4 s. Fix: without an HTTP status (which still decides first: 401/403 `auth`, 404/410 `ended`, 5xx `server`, other 4xx and `HTTP response code said error` without a status `http`), a Video error is `network` when its text (errorStr or errorInfo's `dbgmsg`) names a transfer error other than an HTTP status, an empty playlist, `Errors seen on all bitrates`, a non-zero `last_err`/`lasterror` that is not a status, a reset, refusal, or timeout, or when its code is −1 or −2 (Roku's Video node reference: −1 network error, −2 connection timed out; the client had followed an older listing where 0 was the network error and −1 HTTP's, so 0 stays `network`) or its errorInfo category is `http` (or `network`, which Roku does not document) without a status; a −5 that names none of these is `decode`, and the reducer reads `decode` on a load that has had no media (no segment, no buffer reading above 0, never played) as `network` and logs it. A downloaded segment whose `Status` is non-zero is no longer media (`playback.video.segmentEvent`). Every `network` failure reconnects (`playback.recovery.decide`), before or after the first frame and from any origin: one outage (`state.outage`) runs from the first such failure until a load plays (or the attempt ends), shows "Reconnecting" throughout, sends its first no-op `track_change` at once and each later one, after the plan it brought failed again, after 1, 2, 4, 8, then 15 s; a stall or a media 5xx during the outage continues it, a 401/403 still renews and a decoder error with media still recovers. About two minutes after the outage began the next failure shows "Can't reach the server" with Retry (Try Anyway only while the API is unreachable). Assumed: libcurl's messages and Roku's `last_err` codes other than HTTP statuses mean a failed connection (Roku documents neither; 961 came only with resets); a genuine decoder error comes after some media reached the load, so a −5 with no media would reconnect for two minutes and then show the error instead of trying another route; a dead proxy node is retried through the server's `track_change` answer rather than reported as `sourceRefused`. Not changed: a stall outside an outage keeps the plan's one reopen, then `failure_recovery` with `transport_stall`. Specs replay run 14's strings (URLs replaced by an example): an original's stall, reopen reset, closed retry, and return; a transcode at an explicit quality whose segments and playlists stay refused for the window; a copy remux after playing from its buffer; a stall during the outage; a 401 and a decoder error during it; a seek and Back during the wait. Headless only | Device run: T2a, T2b, T4a, T4b, and T4c again with refusals of 10 s and of 150 s (expect "Reconnecting", `track_change` replans without `failure`, no `plan_failed` route event, playback resuming once the refusal ends, and after 150 s "Can't reach the server" with Retry); a console log of each `Video error` beside the probe's `refuse` lines |
| AG-184 | Probe lab mode / run 14 defect 3 (AG-182 decision 3; extends AG-175) | **Assumed.** `refuse-media.PORT` now also cuts media connections already open when the file appears: every `stream`, `playlist`, and `segment` relay registers with its upstream socket (`MediaTransfers`), and while any is open a watcher thread reads the file every 0.25 s; once it exists, each open relay's upstream socket is shut down (waking a blocked read) and a relay writing to a client that has stopped reading sends in 0.25 s pieces and sees the cut between them, so a cut lands within about 0.5 s. The client's connection then ends as a new refused request's does (TCP RST, or an orderly close for `close`), the upstream is closed, and one `refuse` line (`status` 0, `refusal`, `open: true`, `bytes` relayed with `--extra-facts`) replaces the request's usual line. API and subtitle requests are never cut; relays that ended before the file appeared are not logged; removing the file stops the cutting. Waking a blocked read by shutting its socket down was tested on Linux only; the lab probes run on macOS. Not deployed: each probe must be restarted with its recorded flags | The lab run that uses it: T2a on an original without a seek (expect a `refuse` line with `open: true` for the long range request within 0.5 s of the file, then the client's reconnect) |
| AG-185 | Run 14 fixes / run 14 defect 2 (refines AG-170 and AG-180) | Cause (inferred; BrowseRows logged no focus on the device): W10-4 (`b2ccd23`) reads a report from the focused list while a jump or restoration is pending as the viewer's move unless it is on the jump's card or a place in `m.places` (the first card of new content or of the focused row's new items, and the entry report); run 14's quiet refresh moved Out of Shadows from Continue Watching's third card to its first, the restoration's jump went to the first card, and the ring stayed on the third, on Hidden Figures, which only a list that kept its place across the new content and reported it there (not in `m.places`) can give, since nothing asked for the third card. Fix: `m.places` also holds where the list's ring was, within the new content or the row's new items (`keptPlace`, clamped to them), so that report is the list's; a report anywhere else is still the viewer's move, which ends the jump and the restoration (AG-180), and a debug line (`the viewer's move ends a pending focus restoration`, row and item only) now marks it on the console. Trade-off: a viewer's move onto the card the ring had, inside the window before the jump takes (50–150 ms after a quiet refresh the viewer cannot see coming), reads as the list's and is undone. `FakeRowList.keepsPlace` acts as the inferred list. Specs: T8's return without an early reload, the row's own reload keeping the place, ten T7 cycles from the third card with the place kept or reset and the row reloaded early (run 13) or not (rotating through three titles), T6's Right during the return's jump with either list, and a viewer's card the refresh moves. | AG-186 | Device run 15 (coordinator decisions) | Run 15 (`77f8822`) proved AG-183 on HLS: every outage error (`-5 … last_err:961` on the first refused segment, then `-3 … mpr zero length playlist` on each reconnect plan) was classed `network`, reconnected on the schedule with no-op `track_change`s, and resumed at the same position 8–9 s after a 10 s outage ended; a 150 s outage showed the terminal error with Retry at 2:04, and Retry played about 2 s after the press. Home return focus passed (three reorder returns, ten rotation cycles, Right at 0.2 s twice); node count unchanged. On an original the cut connection produced no `Video` error: Roku played about 88 s from its buffer, then rebuffered, and Auto sent a `quality_change` 6 s later from a bandwidth estimate taken during the outage, so the session came back on a transcode and Retry's start carried the same estimate. Decision: a rebuffer during which no media arrives is an outage, not low bandwidth: Auto never steps down on it (the stall clock and reconnect handle it), and an estimate measured across an outage is discarded rather than sent with a reconnect or Retry | Run 15 fix |
| AG-187 | Run 15 fixes / run 15 defect 1 (AG-186 decision) | Cause: `playback.autoQuality` counted any rebuffer past a load's grace as a bandwidth shortage, whatever reached the node. In T1b the lab cut an original's connection; Roku played 88.7 s from its buffer, then rebuffered with its reading held at 2% (ECP `current="21"` and `"22"` throughout both rebuffers), and 5.7–6.2 s in Auto sent `quality_change` with 70% of the original's 4,026 kbps (2,818), which the server answered with a transcode. The estimate lasts 30 minutes, so the reconnect's `track_change`s and Retry's `start` carried it and the session never went back to the original. Fix: (1) Auto counts a stall only from the first media to arrive in it to the latest (`playback.autoQuality.noteMedia`): a buffer reading above the rebuffer's previous one, a downloaded segment, or the node playing again; the rebuffer's first reading only says where the buffer stands. A rebuffer nothing reaches never counts, so the stall clock's 20 s `transport_stall` (the reopen) and the reconnect path (AG-183) handle it. A stall's count no longer grows without an event, so Auto wakes the reducer only at the end of a hold. (2) Media after an estimate (a rising reading, a segment, or a load playing) confirms it. An unconfirmed estimate is dropped when an outage is noted (`noteOutage`: the reconnect path's first drop, before its first `track_change` is built; a stall clock `stall`; the API unreachable); the estimate it replaced applies again, or none, and its step down sets no hold. Assumed: Roku reports `bufferingStatus` while a rebuffer fills (runs 11 and 12 logged rising readings on slow starts; no rebuffer on a slow link has been logged), so a genuine slow rebuffer shows a rising reading or a segment, and one that shows neither until it plays counts nothing. A gap between arrivals counts, since media still came; 20 s without any is the stall clock's. A step down whose plan fails with a drop before showing media loses its estimate even on a slow link, and Auto steps down again after the next plan's grace if the link is still slow. An Auto `quality_change` already sent keeps its estimate when it is replayed byte for byte. Behaviour change: specs that stepped down with no media now feed rising readings. Specs: run 15's T1b from the ECP readings (no `quality_change`; `no progress for 20 s` with kind `stall` and buffer 2, the reopen, a reset, reconnects and Retry without an estimate); a slow link whose rebuffer fills (a step down at 6 s with 2,818 kbps, kept through a later outage and Retry); a second step down the outage began in (the reconnects and Retry carry the first estimate); an estimate dropped by a 20 s empty start and by an unreachable server. Headless only | Device run: T1b again (an original under Auto, refused until the terminal error, then Retry): expect no `quality_change` during the rebuffer, `no progress for 20 s {"buffer":2,"kind":"stall"}` about 20 s after it began, the reopen, a `network` `Video error`, "Reconnecting", `track_change`s without `bandwidth_estimate_kbps` that plan `original_http`, and Retry's `start` without one, playing the original. If the lab can limit the media rate below the original's bitrate with the API answering, a pass with a temporary info line per `bufferingStatus` reading: expect rising readings in the rebuffer and one Auto `quality_change` about 6 s after the first rise |
| AG-188 | Device run 16 (coordinator decisions) | Run 16 (`36a7da5`) proved AG-187 on an original (the cut range request drained the buffer in 87 s; no `quality_change`; the stall clock reopened after 20 s; `-3 … Transfer error` classed `network`; five no-op `track_change`s on the schedule, none carrying a failure or an estimate; playing again on `original_http` 4.2 s after the outage ended), a regression smoke of every spec 5 area, and a stable node count. Defects: (1) Detail shows Resume (and Start Over) for an item the server marks played with a saved position, but `playback.intent` drops the position when `played` is true, so Resume starts at 0; (2) with Roku's caption mode On, an original MKV shows its embedded SRT while Options says Off (Roku selects the embedded track itself), and the cues show a literal `{\an8}`; (3) one segment request in 918 reached the probe without credential headers during a reanchor start and got a 401, with no error after it (observation). Decisions: (1) Detail and the start intent use one rule for a saved position, following the reference apps for a played item that has a position; (2) the client owns the embedded-track choice on originals: Off means no embedded track is rendered, whatever Roku's caption mode, and a chosen embedded text track is selected explicitly (spec 9.7 to cover embedded tracks); ASS override tags in embedded cues are recorded as a platform limit unless Roku offers a way to hide them; (3) the header-less segment request is watched for in later runs, not fixed | Run 16 fix round |
| AG-189 | Run 16 fixes / run 16 defect 1 (AG-188 decision 1) | Cause: the detail page's `catalog.detail.resumePoint` offered Resume from the saved position whatever `user_data.played` said, while the player's `playback.intent.fromWatch` kept the position only when the item was not played, so a played item with a position showed "Resume 2:19" and started at 0 (and the server's own resume, `resumePositionV3` when `start_position` is omitted, skips completed items too). Rule, from both existing apps: `played` does not decide resume. tvOS's `playableResumePosition` (Detail) and `resolvedStartPosition` (start) and Android TV's `resumePositionSeconds` and `resolvePlaybackStartRequestPosition` read the position alone; the server zeroes the position when it marks an item played (`ResolveProgressState`, over 90%) and keeps the played latch through a rewatch, so a played item with a position is a rewatch stopped partway. One function, `playback.intent.savedPosition` (position past 0 and more than 5 s from the end; `resumeOf` reads a watch projection, its duration being `user_data.duration_seconds`, else the start's version), now decides both the start's `resumeSeconds` and the detail page's Resume, which shows it once past 30 s (`catalog.detail.resumePoint` and `pointOf`, for the movie page, the episode cards, and the watch read). Play on a saved position of 30 s or less still starts there (rewound), as both apps do; the server stores such a position only for items under 10 minutes (its 5% floor, AG-125). `catalog.series.activeEpisode` still skips played episodes when it looks for one in progress (tvOS does the same). Headless only | Device run: a played movie and a played episode rewatched past 5% of the runtime and stopped; Detail's Resume and the episode card's OK start 7 s before the saved position |
| AG-190 | Run 16 fixes / run 16 defect 2 (AG-188 decision 2) | Cause: with Roku's caption mode On and the plan selecting no subtitle, Roku drew a text track embedded in an original MKV, picked by the caption language, while Options said Off. The player only ever kept its own sidecars out of the content (AG-111, AG-115), and a container's tracks cannot be left out. Fix: the `Video` node draws only the plan's subtitle. `playback.sidecars.suppressed(plan, path)` sets `Video.suppressCaptions` whenever the plan selects nothing Roku should draw (off, burned in, or a selected sidecar the content does not carry yet or at all) and clears it only for the selected sidecar on the content, selected by its `TrackName` (the `tmp:/` path the client gave it), or for an HLS rendition; the player writes it before each content goes to the node (`playItem`) and with each subtitle selection (`selectSubtitle`, from an in-place `track_change` or a sidecar that arrives late). `globalCaptionMode` is never written for it (AG-75). Roku documents `suppressCaptions` as a per-node read-write field that "suppresses the closed caption for the purpose of resolving conflicts in cases where UI elements are drawn", and `currentSubtitleTrack` as picked by Roku "when the user has not selected a track" (developer.roku.com, Video node, read 2026-10-23); its closed-caption page documents no other per-video Off and nothing about MKV tracks. A chosen embedded text track keeps arriving as the server's extracted sidecar: the reference apps select embedded tracks natively by an identity their players expose (Android TV `container_track_id`, Media3's Matroska track number; tvOS `ffmpeg_stream_index`, its demuxer's stream index, both through `native_embedded`), but Roku documents no `TrackName` for container tracks, so matching one to the server's identity would be a guess; the client advertises no `native_embedded` and never selects a container track (a pick of one in Roku's overlay is written back, as before). Consequences: Off shows nothing under On, On replay, or On mute, captions carried in the video stream (CEA-608/708, unsupported per spec section 12.5) included; while a chosen embedded track's first extraction runs (about 65 s, A-15) nothing shows rather than Roku's own copy. AG-111's reload when captions come on over carried sidecars is kept as a backstop until hardware proves the suppression. Headless only | Device run: Roku caption mode On, an original MKV with embedded SRT, Subtitles Off (no cue, ECP `captions` empty), then English (the server sidecar, no `{\an8}`), then Off, under On, On replay, and On mute; also Roku's `*` overlay picking a container track. If `suppressCaptions` does not hide container tracks, Roku's documented native `TrackName` for MKV tracks (or proof that it is the Matroska track number) is needed |
| AG-191 | Run 16 fixes / run 16 defect 2 (`{\an8}`) | The literal `{\an8}` came from Roku drawing the MKV's own SRT track, which the client no longer lets it do (AG-190). Roku documents no way to hide ASS override tags and no placement for SRT cues, so this is recorded as a platform limit (spec section 5.9). The server's sidecar paths: its WebVTT conversion (`srtToVTT`) removes every `{\...}` block and turns `\an` into WebVTT cue settings, which the client's WebVTT reader drops, so the cue shows at the bottom; but the original SRT it serves under `subrip_sidecar_v1` (`.srt?original=1`, external and downloaded SRT) keeps the tags for SubRip parsers. The client's SRT reader (`playback.subtitles.srtText`) now removes `{\...}` blocks as FFmpeg and the server do, drops a line left empty and a cue left empty, and keeps an unclosed brace as text. The top placement of such cues is lost on Roku (Android TV's Media3 SubRip decoder keeps it, read from Media3's decoder, not run) | Device run of an external SRT with `{\an8}` cues; a TTML conversion that keeps placement would be a follow-up |
| AG-192 | Device run 17 (coordinator decisions) | Run 17 (`2791211`) proved AG-189 (Resume on a played movie and episode sends the saved position minus 7 s; Start Over 0; a title stopped near its end offers Play) and AG-190/191 (Subtitles Off draws nothing under On, On replay and On mute; English draws the server's sidecar with no literal `{\an8}`; caption mode Off with a track selected draws nothing, as spec 5.9 says), and a stable node count. ECP reports `captions="srt"` in every state, so screenshots of Roku's caption plane are the evidence. Defects: (1) switching between two subtitle tracks in place changes Options and sends the `track_change`, but Roku keeps drawing the track the content was loaded with (4 of 4); (2) On mute is not kept: Roku's caption-mode event reports the effective state (`Off` while unmuted, `On` while muted) after the client writes When mute, so the client read each unmute as the viewer choosing Off and a later mute's `On` reloaded the plan, while device-info read `When mute` throughout. Decisions: (1) an in-place switch between sidecar tracks must make Roku draw the new track (select it among the content's tracks, or reload when no in-place selection exists); (2) the caption-mode setting is read from `roDeviceInfo` (`GetCaptionsMode`) whenever the platform event fires, and the event's value alone never counts as a viewer choice while the setting is When mute; this refines AG-75 and the run 7 fix | Run 17 fix round |
| AG-193 | Run 17 fixes / run 17 defect 1 (AG-192 decision 1) | Cause, inferred and not observed: the player selected a sidecar by writing to `Video.subtitleTrack` the `tmp:/` path it had given as the sidecar's `TrackName` in `SubtitleTracks`, and Roku did not switch. Roku documents `availableSubtitleTracks` as filled first with "the tracks specified in the Content Meta-Data", each entry's `TrackName` as "The track identifier. The value of this field may be used to select the subtitle track", `subtitleTrack` as the selected track's identifier, and `currentSubtitleTrack` as "the identifier of the subtitle track that is playing", which Roku picks by the caption language when none is selected (developer.roku.com, Video node, read 2026-10-23). Roku documents nothing about switching side-loaded tracks during playback or adding tracks to playing content. jellyfin-roku (`components/manager/ViewCreator.bs` and `components/video/VideoPlayerView.bs`, `master` at `72cfe98`, read 2026-10-23) switches side-loaded tracks during playback by writing the listed `TrackName` that contains the URL it supplied, because "Roku mangles the name a bit". Run 17 fits a path that Roku lists under another name: Roku ignored the write and kept its own pick by caption language, the first English track, which was the sidecar selected at load because the client lists it first. Runs 7 and 16 passed Off ↔ track only on titles with one sidecar, where Roku's pick is that sidecar. Fix: the player selects a sidecar by the name Roku lists for it (`playback.sidecars.listedName`: the name equal to the path, else one that contains it; MediaTask's `track<n>.srt` paths never contain one another). Until Roku lists the content's tracks, the player writes the path, then selects again when `availableSubtitleTracks` changes and at the first frame. A switch happens in place only to a carried, ready sidecar that Roku lists, or to Off (`playback.sidecars.inPlace`). Any other switch reloads at the position. Three seconds after an in-place switch, at the next position report, if Roku reports another of the content's sidecars in `currentSubtitleTrack`, the player sends a `track_change` that keeps the plan's selection and reloads (`playback.options.reloadSelection`), once per switch. A Roku overlay pick is matched by the listed name too. Content lists its selected sidecar first, a reopen included. Adding the new sidecar to playing content was not chosen: Roku documents no such addition (the Video node page says only that playlist changes after playback starts are ignored), so a sidecar the content does not carry reloads, as before. The late-arriving selected sidecar (`applySubtitle`) is unchanged and still unproven. Run 17's Forced → English reload is explained by the code, not by a log: English was not converted within the 1 s that a content waits for unselected sidecars (`OTHERS_GRACE_MS`), so the content did not carry it. Development builds log Roku's list ("Roku lists subtitle tracks") and the reload ("Roku did not switch the subtitle in place"). Headless only | Device run on run 17's MKV under captions On: English → Forced → SDH → Forced, each drawing the new track's cues, and the console's listed `TrackName`s. If every switch logs the reload, Roku does not switch side-loaded tracks in place and the 3 s check covers it. If Roku lists the sidecars under names that do not contain their paths, every switch reloads |
| AG-194 | Run 17 fixes / run 17 defect 2 (AG-192 decision 2; supersedes AG-123's On-to-On-mute mapping) | Cause: Main read the caption mode with `roDeviceInfo.GetCaptionsMode()` at launch and on each `isCaptionModeChanged()`. Roku documents that on Roku TVs, "when the user selects "On Mute", this function will return "On" when the TV is muted and "Off" when it is not muted." So run 17's events reported what the TV showed (`Off` for the write while the TV was unmuted, `Off` at each unmute, `On` at each mute) while ECP read `When mute`. AG-123's mapping of `On` to On mute could not cover `Off`. Roku documents `GetCaptionsOption("Mode")` as returning the setting (On, Off, Instant replay, When mute on Roku TVs only), and `roDeviceInfoEvent.GetInfo()`'s `Mode` with the same four values (developer.roku.com, ifDeviceInfo and roDeviceInfoEvent, read 2026-10-23). The coordinator's decision named `GetCaptionsMode`, but Roku's documentation says that call is the source of the defect, so the fix reads the setting with `GetCaptionsOption("Mode")`, from the same `roDeviceInfo` at the same two points. `platform.captionModeEvent(setting, effective)` takes it as the mode when it is one of the four values, else what `GetCaptionsMode()` reports, and keeps the latter as `effective`, which development builds log beside the mode. The player takes the event's mode as the viewer's mode and no longer maps `On` to On mute after its own write (`m.wroteMute` is gone). Mutes and unmutes then report an unchanged When mute and change no plan. A plan without a subtitle reloads without sidecars when On mute is chosen, as for any mode but Off. A subtitle chosen under On mute leaves the mode alone. AppScene's launch value names When mute too, so a title opened later starts with On mute, which AG-123 could not do. The stale-repeat guard (`STALE_CAPTION_MS`, run 7) is kept, because reading the setting does not explain why Roku's first event after a write repeats the old mode. It now covers every Silo write, not only On mute, because run 17 saw `On` or `Instant replay` before `Off` after each Off write. The first event within 3 s that reads the mode before the write is ignored, so a viewer's change through Roku's menu that soon after Silo's write would be missed. Not confirmed on hardware: whether `GetCaptionsOption("Mode")` reads When mute on Roku OS 15.3.4 (the log's `mode` and `effective` show it), and whether the stale first event also comes from the setting read. If `mode` follows `effective`, Roku's documentation is wrong for this TV and the event's `GetInfo().Mode` is the next reading to try |
