| AG-196 | Probe lab mode (AG-182 decision 4; extends AG-175 and AG-184) | **Assumed.** The header probe gains a third control file, `throttle-media.PORT` in `--control-dir`: while it exists, `stream`, `playlist`, and `segment` responses on every listener are relayed to the client no faster than its rate in kbit/s (`3000` is 3 Mbit/s; empty or not a number means 1000; at least 8, at most 1,000,000), on each connection separately. Each relay has a token bucket that starts empty when the throttle begins or resumes, fills at the rate, and sends pieces of 0.1 s of the rate (at least 1 KiB), so a connection receives at most the rate times any stretch plus one piece; response headers are not counted. A request reads the file when it arrives, and AG-184's watcher reads it every 0.25 s for open relays, so an original's single range request slows within about half a second of the file appearing and returns to full speed when it is removed. A `throttle` line (`status` 0, `throttle_kbps`, `open: true` when the file appeared after the request arrived) is logged at each request's first throttled write and is not counted in the summary; later rate changes are not logged. API and subtitle responses are never throttled. `refuse-media` wins: new media requests are refused, and a throttled relay waiting for its bucket still sees a cut within about 0.5 s. The probe reads the upstream only as fast as it writes, so at low rates a server write timeout could end a long answer (logged `error`); whether Silo has one was not checked. Unit tests on Linux loopback: three concurrent relays at 2,000 kbit/s measured 0.97–1.0 of the rate over 2 s; an open relay fell to 0.98 of 1,000 kbit/s within 0.6 s of the file and ran at about 1.1 MB/s after removal. Not deployed; the macOS lab probes need a restart with their recorded flags | The lab run that uses it: W10-1's renewal while buffering (AG-182 decision 4), and AG-187's slow-link pass with a rate below the original's bitrate and the API answering (expect one `throttle` line per media request, rising `bufferingStatus` readings in the rebuffer, and one Auto `quality_change` about 6 s after the first rise); or throttled answers end in `error` lines at the rates used |
| AG-197 | W11 review / W11-1 (refines AG-183) | Cause: `playback.session.handleFailure` read every `decode` on a load without media (`loadMedia` false: no segment, no buffer reading above 0, never played) as `network`. An explicit decoder diagnosis before any callback, such as −5 `mediaerror` `decoder:video:unsupported profile` on an original (which has no HLS segment to report), therefore reconnected with no-op `track_change`s for two minutes and ended in "Can't reach the server"; the server never heard of the decoder failure. `loadMedia` records the callbacks Roku delivered, not what its demuxer consumed. Fix: `playback.video.failure`'s table, in order: (1) an HTTP status: 401/403 `auth`; 404/410 `ended` from the configured origin, `http` from a proxy; 5xx `server`; other 4xx and `HTTP response code said error` `http` (unchanged); (2) a failed connection, `network`: code −1, −2, or 0, category `http` or `network`, `Transfer error:` other than an HTTP status, `zero length playlist`, `Errors seen on all bitrates`, a non-zero `last_err`/`lasterror` that is not a status, a reset, refusal, or close, `couldn't connect`/`resolve`, a timeout (unchanged); (3) **new row:** a −5 whose errorStr or `dbgmsg` names a decoder, a codec, or something unsupported (`\bdecod`, `\bcodec`, `unsupported`) is `decode`, which the reducer keeps whatever reached the load (`failure_recovery` `decoder_failure`); (4) **new row:** any other −5 is `media`; (5) anything else `player`. The reducer (`mediaKind`) reads `media` as `decode` once the load has had media; before any, as `network` once (a reconnect; `attempt.mediaRetried`, cleared when a load plays) and for as long as the API does not answer; after that retry, with the API answering, as `decode`, which ends the outage (the connection answered) and sends `decoder_failure`. Runs 14–16's strings keep rows 1–2, and their specs pass unchanged. Assumed: Roku names the decoder or the format when its decoder refuses a stream (the decoder text in the specs is synthetic: no run has captured one); a −5 that names nothing, after media or a second time before media with the API answering, is the decoder's, so a refused connection that Roku reported as a bare −5 (none seen: run 15's refusals were `last_err:961`) would exclude its route after one retry; unknown non-HTTP `last_err` codes stay connection evidence (only 961 seen, with resets); a −3 naming a decoder stays `player`. Headless only | Device run: a file the TV cannot decode (if the lab has one), expecting the console's `Video error` text, `failure_recovery` with `decoder_failure`, and no "Reconnecting"; T1c and T2a refusals again, expecting run 15's and 16's sequences |
| AG-198 | W11 review / W11-2 (refines AG-183) | Cause: the reconnect budget (`RECONNECT_BUDGET_MS`, 120 s) was checked only when a load failed again (`startReconnect`) or a reconnect replan got no answer (`retryLater`). A reconnect whose load buffered without playing, with a segment below a full buffer every few seconds restarting the stall clock, failed neither way; adoption had cleared the outage's next try, so nothing woke the reducer for the budget and "Reconnecting" could last indefinitely. A load still waiting for the Video node (the stall clock waits for assignment) or a replan the server held did the same. Fix: the outage ends at `startedAt + RECONNECT_BUDGET_MS`, from its first drop across every replan and reload it brings until a load plays. `nextWake` includes that time, and `playback.session.expireOutage` checks it before each event except Back and an identity change, which end the session without the error. At expiry it shows `connection_lost` with Retry once (Try Anyway while the API is unreachable) and retires the attempt, so a later replan answer, `assigned`, buffering, input, or first frame is not the session's, and the outage's wake goes with it; Retry starts at the position the attempt had, a seek during the outage included. The two budget checks it replaces are gone. Behaviour change: the error now comes at two minutes after the outage began instead of at the first failure or unanswered try after it, so a try due at that moment gives way to the error: run 15's T1d and T1f sent 12 `track_change`s and showed the error 2:04.2 and 2:04.6 after the outage began (the twelfth try's plan failing a few seconds after it went at 2:00); the same refusal now sends 11 and shows it at 2:00, and the T4a spec replaying it says so. Assumed: a load that has not played two minutes after the drop is not worth waiting for even while its buffer fills (a slow link gets its first frame well within that). Headless only | Device run: T1d's 150 s refusal (expect 11 `track_change`s and the error with Retry 2:00 after the first `Video error`, then Retry playing); with the lab's `throttle-media` (AG-196) at a rate too low for the reconnect's load to start, the error at 2:00 instead of "Reconnecting" without end |
| AG-199 | W11 review / W11-3 (refines AG-193) | Cause: the player's check three seconds after an in-place subtitle switch (`checkSubtitle`, AG-193) was consumed at the first position report past its time even when it returned early because another track change was on its way (`playback.session.changingTracks`). If the server then refused that change, the plan kept the switched-to sidecar while Roku drew the one before, nothing checked again, and Options named a track Roku did not draw. Fix: the check stays pending while a track change is in flight or waiting. A refusal (the plan stays) leaves it to the next position report, which reloads the plan's selection if Roku still reports another carried sidecar; an accepted change replaces it: a retrack arms a check of its own selection, and a new load drops it (`playItem`), as before. The one reload per switch stands: the check is consumed when it reloads, finds the switch drawn, or its load is gone. Not changed: a quality or output change in flight does not hold the check (its plan reloads anyway, and the check then sees another load). Specs drive a PlayerProbe through the production reducer's refusal (`probeRefuse`) and through the reload's new content; they supply the unfollowed switch, which run 18 never saw on the reference TV. Headless only | Device run: on run 17's MKV under captions On, English → Forced, then SDH within 3 s while the lab's `hold-replan` (AG-175) holds that replan for 10 s; expect no reload before its answer, SDH drawn after it, and Options and the drawn cues agreeing. The lab cannot make the server refuse a track change, so the refusal path stays headless |
| AG-200 | Device run 19 (coordinator decisions) | Run 19 (`9ab4b23`) proved the two-minute outage budget (11 reconnects, the error at 120.0 s after the first drop, also under a 50 kbit/s throttle; Retry played about 2 s after the key), a real decoder error (`Video error -5: :pump:Unsupported video format: AOMedia Video 1 codec:…` classed `decode`, `failure_recovery` `decoder_failure`, the server moved to a transcode with no reconnects), a genuine slow-link step-down (rising buffer readings, one `quality_change` 7.6 s in, then steady), a renewal 2.7 s into a rebuffer with no step-down (lab tokens set to 3 minutes and restored to 1 hour, verified), a subtitle switch under a held replan, bitmap burn-in (Off and On only) and unstyled ASS, the copy-remux start target (14 starts: median 1.71 s, max 2.39 s from keypress to `VODStartComplete` in a `perf` build, re-resumes 1.4–1.6 s), and a stable node count. Defect: when a subtitle sidecar arrives after an original is playing, `applySubtitle` rewrites `subtitleTracks` on the playing content, Roku closes the player, and the stall clock later reads the closed player's full buffer as `no_output` and excludes a working original. Decisions: (1) the client never changes the tracks of content that is playing; a sidecar that arrives late is applied with one reload at the current position (AG-193's uncarried-sidecar rule); (2) a player the client itself closed or reloaded is never evidence of a decoder failure; (3) the 8 s start target is met on this TV for all three routes (run 10 for originals and transcodes, run 19 for copy remuxes); the first play from 0 before a keyframe index still starts 8 s late (AG-153, a server item) | Run 19 fix |
| AG-201 | Run 19 fixes / run 19 defect 1 (AG-200 decisions 1 and 2) | Cause: a selected sidecar that became ready after its content went onto the `Video` node was added to that content: `applySubtitle` rewrote the playing content's `SubtitleTracks` and selected the sidecar. Roku then listed only that track and closed the player (ECP `close`), and the reducer, not told, kept its stall clock on the closed load: FX-028 (playing; the sidecar 28.8 s after the pick) ended in `no progress for 20 s` `stall` and the plan's one reopen; S3E1 (still buffering; 5.0 s; a full buffer reading before the close) in `no_output`, `failure_recovery` `decoder_no_output`, and a playable original excluded. Fix: (1) content on the node is never changed. The player sends the reducer `subtitleReady` (`{ load, tracks }`, tracks from `playback.options.reloadSelection`; `lateSubtitle`), which holds it on the plan (`subtitleReload`) and, once no replan is in flight or queued, no reconnect runs, and the load has not failed (`settleSubtitle`), reloads the plan once at the position: a local `reload`, the renewal's (not the plan's one transport reopen), whose new content carries the sidecar the player kept, where `playback.seek.decide` allows one; else (a copy remux) a `track_change` that keeps the selection (`inPlace` false, AG-193's reload), whose new content downloads the sidecar again, at most once per subtitle in an attempt (`attempt.subtitleReplans`), so a sidecar late again is logged and shows after the next reload rather than reloading in a loop. Any replan's new plan drops the held reload (a viewer's track change, which supersedes it, a quality change, a reconnect: each new content carries its own selection; an in-place answer makes its own selection), a refusal leaves it to follow, and a failed load's recovery reload carries the sidecar and clears it. Content handed to the slot but still waiting for the node's stop is not on the node and takes the sidecar before it plays, as before. Until the reload, the content plays without the subtitle (`suppressCaptions`, AG-190). (2) A state the client causes is never stall evidence: every reload, content swap, or stop it makes either starts a new load in the reducer first (`reload` or `adopt`: `loadAssigned` false and the clock restarted, then started again at the new content's `assigned`, AG-159) or leaves the active phase (Back, the error), and the replaced content's callbacks carry its old load, which the reducer ignores. Other writes audited, all safe by that rule or as selections on playing content: `Video.content` only in `playItem`, for a load the reducer started; `control` stop only from the slot (for such a load, leaving, or the error), pause and resume (the reducer stops the clock while paused); `seek` only from reducer commands, each restarting the clock; `subtitleTrack`, `audioTrack` (the overlay's write-back), `suppressCaptions`, `globalCaptionMode`, and Up Next's box, selections Roku documents for playing content (run 18 proved in-place subtitle switches); content fields (`playStart` in `seekLoad`, `SubtitleTracks` in `playItem` and `lateSubtitle`) only while the content waits for the stop. Not changed: a stop Roku makes on its own still reaches the clock as before; none is known but run 19's. Specs replay both run 19 sequences through the reducer (the replaced content's full buffer and 21 s without the new content give no `no_output`) and the player through a PlayerProbe (the playing content keeps its tracks; the reload carries the sidecar; Off in place, or a new plan, before it arrives supersedes it). Headless only | Device run under captions On, English (ASS) picked during play on FX-028 and S3E1: "Loading subtitles…", play without cues, then at the sidecar `the subtitle came after its content started; the plan reloads at the position`, `content assigned after the stop`, Roku listing the sidecar beside the content's others, cues from the position, and no `no progress for 20 s` or `failure_recovery`; on an embedded ASS track over a copy remux, one `track_change` instead |
| AG-202 | Device run 20 (coordinator decisions) | Run 20 (`fb4c489`) proved AG-201's late path on a copy remux (a sidecar 78 s late caused one `track_change`, no loop), a viewer's Off winning over a pending sidecar, and a playback regression on all three routes (skips, ladder, audio and subtitle changes, a 10 s outage, pause, Back, Resume, Up Next, Home return focus); node count plateau as in runs 17–19. Defect: on MKV originals with built-in ASS tracks, about 1 s after content carrying the selected sidecar starts, Roku lists its tracks again and reports the file's own track (`mkv/3`) as current; the client's switch check then selects the sidecar again (Roku already reports it selected under its bare path), Roku stops (`Video.state` `finished` 0.17 s later) and stays black until the stall clock reads `no_output` and excludes the original (4 of 5 loads); a build that skipped that selection played 2 of 2 with cues. Decisions: (1) the switch check compares track identity by the sidecar's path (Roku's listed name contains it, and its bare-path report counts), never re-selects a track Roku reports as selected, and never writes `subtitleTrack` in the first seconds after content starts unless the viewer chose a different track; (2) a `finished` state that follows the client's own `subtitleTrack` write, before the content's end, is client-caused: it reloads at the position and is never evidence of a decoder failure (AG-200 decision 2) | Run 20 fix |
| AG-203 | Run 20 fix / run 20 defect 1 (AG-202 decisions 1 and 2) | Cause, inferred from T1f's two builds and not proven: on MKV originals with ASS tracks, the player selected the carried sidecar by its listed name when Roku first listed the content's tracks (before the content started, `subtitleTrack` read empty); Roku then reported `subtitleTrack` as the bare `tmp:/` path. About 1 s after the content started Roku listed the tracks again with the file's own (`mkv/3` to `mkv/9`) and reported `currentSubtitleTrack` `mkv/3`; `onVideoSubtitles` compared the listed name with the bare path, found them different, and selected the sidecar again while the node buffered; 0.17 s later `bufferingStatus` read 100 and `Video.state` became `finished` before any frame. `playback.video.stateEvent` drops a `finished` from content that never played (harness defect 3), so the stall clock kept the stopped load and sent `no_output` 20 s later. Fix (1), selection: `playback.sidecars.isTrack(name, path)` is the identity Roku reports a sidecar by, the path or any name containing it (`listedName`, the overlay's match in `playback.options.overlaySubtitle`, and the 3 s check use it; `trackPath` names the carried sidecar a reported name is, or none). The player's own selections (when Roku lists the tracks, at the first frame, after the settle time; `ensureSubtitle`) never write a sidecar Roku's `subtitleTrack` reports selected, and log Roku's `currentSubtitleTrack` when it is a track Roku found in the stream. From the content's start (its first `buffering`, `playing`, or `paused`) until `SUBTITLE_SETTLE_MS` (3 s) after its first frame the player writes nothing on its own (`holdsSubtitle`): a selection still needed is made once at the first position report after it, and a change Roku makes to `subtitleTrack` meanwhile that the overlay would put back waits for it too. Before the content starts its setup selection stands (the listed name when Roku reports none), and a viewer's switch (`retrack`) is written when it comes. The 3 s check reloads only when Roku reports another of the content's sidecars as playing, by any name containing its path (a bare path of the sidecar before, which it missed, now counts), never a track Roku found in the stream, and it always falls after the settle time because a switch in place needs content that has played. Fix (2), client-caused stops: the player notes when it changes started content (`noteWrite`: `subtitleTrack`, `audioTrack`, `suppressCaptions`, the caption mode; seeks and pause are not counted, since a seek can reach the end), and a `finished` within `WRITE_STOP_MS` (2 s) carries `afterWrite`, played or not. The reducer (`stoppedByClient`) then reloads the plan at the position as a renewal does, a local `reload` or else a `seek_reanchor`, not the plan's one reopen, and the new load restarts the stall clock; a `finished` within `END_SLACK_SECONDS` (5 s) of the plan's runtime still ends the session, and so does one without `afterWrite` from content that played, as before. After such a stop the player writes nothing on its own on that plan's started content again (`m.quietPlan`, the plan's `attemptId`), so a write Roku keeps refusing cannot reload in a loop. Assumed, not observed: that Roku draws the sidecar it reports selected under its bare path without the first-frame selection that T1f's probe build still made, after which its cues drew (if cues do not draw, the next step is that one selection after the settle time while Roku reports `mkv/3`); that `mkv/3` in `currentSubtitleTrack` while `subtitleTrack` names the sidecar does not mean Roku draws the file's track; that 3 s after the first frame is past Roku's own track changes on these files; that a client write is the only cause of a `finished` that soon (a real failure in that window would reload once rather than reach the clock); that without a runtime a `finished` after a write is not the end (a reload at the real end plays out to it again). | AG-204 | Device run 21 (coordinator decisions) | Run 21 (`2f7f4a1`) proved run 20's stop is gone (9 of 9 cached-sidecar loads on MKV originals with built-in ASS played and drew English, with no failure lines and no selection after the first frame), in-place switches among English, Arabic and Off (nine, each drawing its own track; Roku's caption font has no Arabic glyphs, so Arabic shows boxes — a platform limit), a late sidecar arriving before the first frame, and a stable node count. | AG-205 | Run 21 fix / run 21 defect 1 (AG-204 decisions 1 to 3) | Cause: AG-203 made the player leave any track Roku found in the stream that `currentSubtitleTrack` reported while `subtitleTrack` named the sidecar (`left as it is`), and it selected nothing after the first frame once Roku reported the sidecar selected. Fix: (1) at the first position report after the settle time (`SUBTITLE_SETTLE_MS`, 3 s after the first frame; `confirmSubtitle`), the player checks once per load what Roku reports (`playback.sidecars.settled`): if `subtitleTrack` does not name the sidecar, or `currentSubtitleTrack` names another sidecar or a track Roku found in the stream, it writes the sidecar's listed name once (`selectSubtitle`, which notes the write). `SUBTITLE_CHECK_MS` (3 s) after that write it looks again and only logs a track Roku still plays instead (`Roku still plays another subtitle track after the player selected the sidecar; left as it is`): no further write or reload on that load. A `finished` within `WRITE_STOP_MS` of it is AG-203's client-caused reload, whose content carries the choice and gets its own check; the player counts such stops per selected subtitle (`m.writeStops`, by track ID rather than plan, since a `seek_reanchor` reload brings a new plan) and after `WRITE_STOPS_QUIET` (2) in a row makes no selection of its own for that subtitle until the viewer chooses a track (`trackChosen`). This replaces AG-203's `m.quietPlan`, which ended the player's own selections on a plan after one such stop. (2) A track Roku found in the stream is accepted, logged, and not corrected when it is the only stream track Roku lists in the sidecar's language (`playback.tracks.languageKey`) and Roku reports the sidecar selected: the English choice drawn from the file's own English track (run 21 T1, all nine loads). Two stream tracks in the language (S3E1's two Spanish), a sidecar without a language, or no listing mean a write. Accepting any same-language track was not chosen: a file with English and English Signs, or two Spanish variants, would keep the wrong one. (3) Development builds log each `subtitleTrack` write (`subtitleTrack written`, its value) and Roku's selected and playing tracks at each listing, track change, the first frame, the settle check, and the look after the write (`Roku's subtitle tracks`), at debug level; values are listed names, `tmp:/` paths, or stream track names, and a value containing `://` is masked. Not changed: the setup selection while content is set up, the hold from the content's start to the settle time (run 20's no-early-write rule), the in-place switch check (`checkSubtitle`); a viewer's switch in place within the settle time consumes the load's settle check, since the switch has its own. The hold's waiting selection (`m.subtitlePending`) is folded into the settle check: a change Roku makes to `subtitleTrack` during the hold is corrected there. Assumed, not observed: that one write of the listed name after the settle time makes Roku draw the chosen sidecar on these files (run 20's write at the second listing stopped the content; this one comes after the first frame, and a stop reloads); that Roku's English `mkv/3` is the track the English sidecar was converted from. | AG-206 | Device run 22 (coordinator decisions) | Run 22 (`35bf1c2`) ran on the owner's own development server at the owner's request (no lab, no header probe; plans routed through a proxy node), on two anime MKV originals with several built-in English ASS tracks and SRT sidecars in seven languages. Decisions: (1) drop AG-205 decision 2: `currentSubtitleTrack` naming a stream track in the right language is not evidence that Roku draws it, so after the settle window a stream track Roku plays while a sidecar is chosen always gets the one corrective write, whatever its language; (2) the in-place check must recognise a proxy-routed `track_change` plan for the same media: compare what stays the same across the plans the server issues for one media (traced from the server's proxy URL scheme), never a client-made identity, and keep the reload when the media really changes; (3) Back closes an open Options panel on the first press at any time, including across a reload, and a track pick that reloads closes the panel as other picks do; (4) a subtitle sidecar written to `tmp:/` gets its own size budget, set from the largest sidecars the client must handle and the memory the client's SRT reading takes on a 512 MB device, recorded with its reason; JSON keeps 2 MiB; (5) committed evidence from the owner's server names titles by class only; titles and positions played stay in the private evidence notes so the owner can reset watch state | Run 22 fix |

| AG-208 | Run 22 fix / run 22 defect 2 (AG-206 decision 2) | Cause, traced in the server source (silo-server `roku/client-support` at `c166d16d1` and `origin/main` at `087aaf2c1`, read only; the proxy URL code is the same on both): an attempt without `header_authenticated_media_v1` (D-1) that the server routes through a proxy node gets `original_http` as `{proxy}/stream/direct/{token}` (`playback_v3.go` builds it from `ClientURLFor`), where the token is an HS256 stream token (`streamtoken.Sign`) carrying the session, file, and egress node, with `iat` and `exp` (24 h) to the second, so two signings differ unless they fall in the same second. Every replan, `track_change` included, runs the identity transport again (`prepareIdentityTransportV3`) and signs a new token; `PlanRoute` re-picks the proxy round-robin. `playback.sidecars.sameMedia` compared the stream URL with only `st` removed (AG-109 kept a proxy path token on purpose), so every plan was another stream and every switch reloaded, while local seeks, which need no plan, stayed local. The run 22 console's plan lines read `"origin":"proxy"`, `original_http`, `http_progressive`, `mkv` on all the reloads. The old URL stays good: the proxy's `handleDirectPlay` checks the token's signature and expiry, that its egress node is this node (`proxyEgressStatusV3`), and the session-deny marker, which the server sets only when the session stops (`StreamDeny`); nothing revokes a direct-play token at a replan. Fix: `playback.media.withoutStreamToken` also drops the last path segment after `/stream/direct/` of an absolute URL (the proxy's direct-play route, `DIRECT_ROUTE`), so two plans for the same file on the same node compare equal; the origin (another proxy node, or the configured origin), the route, `seek`, the plan fields AG-109 compares (file, delivery, protocol, container, recipe, transformations, audio, timeline), and the token of a proxy's remux (`/stream/remux/{token}`, whose job the server stops and replaces at a replan) or transcode route still count. An HLS replan that only changes a sidecar already copies the old `stream` byte for byte (`sidecarOnlyHLSReplanV3`). Nothing client-made is compared. The retrack keeps the content's URL and its token's expiry (`urlExpiresAt`, and `credentialExpiresAt` for a proxy), so the token renewal of AG-140 and the one-hour URL margin apply to the kept URL as before. Not changed: grant mode (`/stream/v3/{session_id}`), which the client does not negotiate. Specs: proxy-shaped URL and plan fixtures (`proxy.example`, fake tokens): the same original on one node with renewed tokens matches (Off → English → Off retracks and keeps the first URL); another node, the configured origin, another audio track, another file, a remux, or a renewed remux token does not; the API-origin `st` cases are unchanged. Server follow-up drafted in spec section 14: keep an original's proxy node on an intent replan, since with two or more proxies a switch can still reload. Run 22's key log fits that alone: a pick, a Back 1 s later, and the next Back two minutes later closed it (the console logs no focus change across the reload; every focus change goes through `applyFocus`, which leaves an open panel's focus alone). The reload did not cause it, it only made the open panel more visible over new content. Reference apps, read from source and not run: tvOS's `TVPlayerInfoHUD` dismisses on Menu from anywhere in the HUD (`.onExitCommand(perform: onDismiss)`), and Android TV's `TvPlayerHud` closes only a pop-up picker first, else the HUD (`onPreviewKeyEvent`, `BackHandler`); in both a track pick leaves the HUD open (`selectSubtitle`, `selectSubtitleOption`). So "as other picks do" is: the panel stays open after a pick, in place or reloading, as now. Fix: the panel's Back always sets `closed` (the `backed` field is gone; the player observes `closed`); the controls reducer's Back closes Options when the panel has lost focus, with its list focused or not (its `closePicker` command, the player's `closePicker`, and the panel's `onPicker` are gone; `picker` still keeps the keys with the panel). Spec 5.7's Back chain loses "close an open picker". Specs: the panel closes with one Back from a list after a pick and from the tabs; the reducer closes Options with a list focused; the player keeps the panel open and focused across a track pick that reloads (a queued `track_change` without `inPlace`, new content to its first frame) and closes it on one Back. The console logged no size (the warnings carried status and time only), so the file is known only to be over 2 MiB. The server serves a `render` decision's embedded ASS as extracted, in its own format (`attachSubtitleArtifactV3`; only `convert` gets WebVTT), and caps no extraction (its subtitle cache is 2 GiB in all; uploads 5 MB, provider downloads 16 MiB), so a typeset anime track arrives at full size; the client may not ask for another URL (protocol section 4.2). Budget: a download (`api.request` `download`, used only for subtitle sidecars saved to `tmp:/downloads`) gets `api.response.MAX_SIDECAR_BYTES`, 8 MiB (`api.response.limit`), while it downloads (the running file's size and a declared `Content-Length`) and when kept (`saved`); JSON bodies, a download's error body included, keep 2 MiB. Why 8 MiB: the cue limit (`MAX_CUES`, 20,000) already bounds what can show, and 8 MiB is 20,000 ASS lines of about 420 bytes, the size of motion-tracked sign lines (positions, clips, transforms); a larger file is almost always over the cue limit anyway, and each extra MiB costs `tmp:/` space and download time for nothing shown. Memory: MediaTask read the whole file (`ReadAsciiFile`), and `lines` made a carriage-return-free copy and split it while the original stayed referenced, about three times the file before any cue (some 24 MiB for 8 MiB). It now reads the file `playback.subtitles.CHUNK_BYTES` (256 KiB) at a time (`roByteArray.ReadFile` with an offset), each piece cut back to its last line feed so no line or UTF-8 sequence is split, and feeds whole lines to a reader (`newReader`, `feed`, `finish`; `convert` feeds the whole text, as before); a line longer than a chunk fails `too_large`; ASS regexes are built once per file, not three per line; `shift` moves the cues in place instead of copying them; a conversion past `MAX_CUES` drops its cues at once. Estimated peak, not measured on a device (H17 has not measured the 512 MB player's limit): about 1.5 MiB for a chunk as bytes, text, and lines; at 20,000 cues about 7 MiB of cue objects (assuming about 300 bytes per three-member object with its text) and about 6 MiB while `toSrt` builds and joins the SRT; about 15 MiB at most, for any file within the budget. The 2 MiB budget already admitted a 20,000-cue SRT, whose whole-file read peaked higher (about 19 MiB by the same estimate), so the new peak is below what was accepted before. `tmp:/` holds each download until MediaTask converts it (one at a time) and deletes it, then its SRT (1.2 MB for 20,000 typeset lines); with four sidecars each at the budget, up to 32 MiB of downloads could wait at once, but a content carries one selected sidecar and its inventory's others, usually external SRTs. A synthetic 6.3 MiB typeset ASS (19,990 lines) converted to 19,990 cues in 23 s under brs-node (CPU on Roku not measured; "Loading subtitles…" shows meanwhile and a late sidecar reloads once, AG-201). The `too_large` warnings now carry the size received or declared (`size`). Specs: the budget per request, a sidecar past 2 MiB kept and one past 8 MiB refused, a download's error body judged at 2 MiB, text fed a line at a time or in larger pieces converting as the whole text, and a file read in chunks (multibyte text, byte order mark, CRLF) matching the whole-text result, with an overlong line failing `too_large` | Device run of file A's Auto subtitle (the built-in full English ASS) on the owner's server or a lab title with a typeset ASS track over 2 MiB: no `too_large`, "Loading subtitles…", then cues (positions dropped), and the `api` and `media` lines with no failure; on the 512 MB player, the platform memory percentage before and after the conversion. No URL, host, path, query, or token is logged; the player now passes reducer logs at `debug` level to `log.debug`, which release builds compile out. Specs: every refusal path names its condition (not carried, not played, failed content, a credential past its refresh time, a kept URL under an hour, another file, another stream with every failing check by name, another proxy node with `sameOrigin` false), and the run 23 replay checks that no logged line contains a host, path, token, or `://`. | AG-213 | Run 23 fix / run 23 defect 2 (AG-211 decision 2) | Cause, from the server source (silo-server `roku/client-support` at `c166d16d1` and `origin/main` at `5ec820486`, read only; the code is the same on both) and the client code: (a) proven, and the only cause the source supports: `PlanRoute` picks a direct-play route's proxy by round-robin (`internal/nodepool/planner.go` `pickProxy`, `p.rr[rrKey] % len(candidates)`) under one counter key (`""`) that every client's v3 starts and replans, HLS rehomes, jellycompat playback, and theme songs share (and, on `origin/main`, speed-test probes); a replan deletes the session's reservation and keeps no egress identity (`planner.go` `PlanRoute`; `playback_v3.go`: "A replan starts without an egress identity"). So with two or more proxies a subtitle switch keeps the content only when its plan lands on the content's node, which other traffic on the server decides: run 23's 3 of 11, with no timing pattern, fits that; the client cannot see the node (it logs no URL), so the number of proxies on the owner's server is inferred, not observed. (b) disproved: a plan's `expires_at` is `NewPlanExpiryV3` (now + `MaxTokenTTL`, a fixed 24 h, `protocol_v3.go`, `recipecard.go`), and a proxy URL's path token is signed with the same 24 h (`streamtoken.Sign`); `auth.access_token_expiry` appears in no playback, plan, or URL code. On the client a proxy plan's credential is its `expires_at` (`planState`), `onCredentials` ignores headers for a proxy stream, and the account session's `mediaHeaders` gives no credential for another origin, so the access token's expiry never shortens a proxy URL's margin. (c) disproved: an `original_http` replan never reuses its transport (`sidecarOnlyHLSReplanV3` is HLS only), and between two plans of a subtitle-only `track_change` on the same file only the path token (and the host, when the node moves), `expires_at`, `plan_id`, `plan_attempt_key`, `source_start_seconds`, `player_start_seconds`, the subtitle decision, and `claims.subtitles` change; `delivery`, protocol, container, `effective_recipe`, `transformations`, `selected_tracks.audio` (the same `{ id, index }`), `stream_origin_seconds` and `timeline_offset_seconds` (0), and the inventory's sidecar URLs stay. Sidecar URLs are relative API paths (`/api/v2/stream/{session}/subtitles/{n}.{ext}?file_id=…`) with no token of the plan's, and whether a content carries a sidecar is matched by its track ID (`playback.sidecars.pathOf`), never its URL. No client change beyond AG-212: another node is another transport and reservation, so it still reloads (AG-208), and AG-208's server ask (spec section 14) covers it. A subtitle choice can still change a plan's route (burn-in falls back to a remux or a transcode, a `track_change` may pick another version of the file); those plans reload as before. Specs replay run 23 T2 with proxy-shaped fixtures (`proxy-a.example`, `proxy-b.example`, fake tokens, `expires_at` 24 h on) under a 5-minute access token reported in the media headers: file B's six switches all retrack and keep the first URL and its expiry when every plan stays on one node; file A's five switches retrack, reload, reload, reload, retrack when the plans alternate nodes as round-robin would, each reload logging only `stream_origin` and `sameOrigin` false; `mediaDifferences` is empty for a subtitle-only replan built as the server builds one. | AG-214 | Run 23 fix / run 23 defect 1 (AG-211 decision 3) | Cause: file A's Auto subtitle, its built-in full English ASS, is 10,736,314 bytes as served, over AG-210's 8 MiB, so ApiTask refused it (`too_large`) on all 13 starts before MediaTask saw it. Budget: a download's budget is decided when it starts (`api.response.limit(request, roomKb)`, `sidecarBudget`, kept on ApiTask's transfer entry for the size checks, the declared length, and `saved`): an eighth (`FREE_SHARE` 8) of the memory the app has free (`roAppMemoryMonitor.GetChannelAvailableMemory`, KB), at most `MAX_SIDECAR_BYTES` (16 MiB) and at least `MAX_BODY_BYTES` (2 MiB); 8 MiB (`UNKNOWN_ROOM_SIDECAR_BYTES`) when the reading is missing or not positive. Why free memory, not `GetVolumeInfo("tmp:")`: Roku's ifFileSystem page says the call "can only be called on external volumes; internal volumes do not return meaningful information", and its File System page says files in `tmp:` increase the app's memory usage, so the room `tmp:` has is the app's free memory; ApiTask still reads `GetVolumeInfo("tmp:/")` (`free-blocks` × `blocksize`) and logs it beside the budget and `availableKb` (debug `api: download budget`), never to decide. Why 16 MiB: half again file A's size, the only large track measured; conversion time grows with the file (the synthetic file below: 2.4 MB in about 4 s under brs-node; Roku not measured), so a larger cap buys minutes of "Loading subtitles…" for typesetting the conversion drops. Why an eighth: ApiTask keeps up to four downloads (`MAX_DOWNLOADS`), each judged against the free memory it saw, so together they hold at most half of it, leaving the rest to the conversion (about 15 MiB at the cue limit, AG-210's estimate) and the player; run 23's TV had 515,284 KB free at launch (an eighth is 62.9 MiB, so 16 MiB applies), and file A fits from 83,878 KB free. Why the 2 MiB floor: no reading refuses what every response may hold. Why 8 MiB when unknown: AG-210's budget, held by the same TV in runs 22 and 23 (which downloaded the 10.24 MiB file whole before refusing it). Conversion: `playback.subtitles.feed` now takes pieces cut anywhere but inside a character, holding the text after the last line feed for the next piece (`pending`); `convertFile` cuts each 256 KiB chunk at its last whole UTF-8 character (`wholeCharacters`, at most four bytes read; non-UTF-8 bytes decode as a whole read does) instead of popping bytes back to a line feed, which under brs-node took 83 s for a 1.6 MB file of 4 KB drawing lines (index access copies there; Roku not measured). ASS: an event's text keeps what lies outside a drawing (`\p1` or higher until `\p0`), drops its override blocks, and an event with no text left is dropped and counted (`dropped`); an event whose text matches one of the last eight cues kept and starts within 50 ms of its end lengthens that cue (`merged`: a sign tracked per frame, or drawn in layers, is one cue, and stacked copies would show twice); a line longer than a chunk, which in ASS only a drawing produces, is skipped to its end and counted dropped (SRT and WebVTT still fail `too_large`). So memory holds at most a chunk's text and the cues kept (`MAX_CUES` 20,000), whatever the file's size. MediaTask, unchanged, deletes the download after converting it. Development builds log `media: subtitle converted` with the format, file size, cues kept, dropped and merged events, and error. File A's cue count after dropping is unknown: if its signs and dialogue keep more than 20,000 cues it fails `too_many_cues` and still shows "Subtitles are too large to show." Specs: the budget at run 23's free memory, 128 MiB, 100 MiB, 16 MiB, and past Integer's range, the 8 MiB fallback for missing readings, `saved` against the budget a download started with (run 23's file kept at 16 MiB, refused at 8 MiB); drawings, karaoke, positioned signs, tags-only events, `\pbo`, and an unclosed block; per-frame, layered, and interleaved signs merged and repeated dialogue kept; a synthetic 2.4 MB typeset ASS generated in the test (450 blocks of a 4 KB drawing, a fade, a ten-frame sign, a two-layer sign, karaoke, and two dialogue lines) converted in chunks to 2,250 cues with 900 dropped and 4,500 merged and no vector commands; an overlong ASS line skipped and an SRT one refused; chunks cut inside multibyte and Latin-1 characters matching the whole read. Headless only | Device run of file A's Auto subtitle on the owner's server: `api: download budget` with a positive `availableKb` and a 16 MiB budget (and what `tmpFreeBytes` reads), no `too_large`, `media: subtitle converted` with the file's size, kept, dropped, and merged counts, then cues; `chanperf` before and after the conversion. If `availableKb` is missing in ApiTask's Task thread the budget is 8 MiB and the file still fails: then the reading has to come from another thread |
| AG-215 | Run 23 fix / run 23 observation (AG-211 decision 4) | Cause: a notice (`notice`, 4 s, `NOTICE_MS`) expired by the clock from when it was raised, and the status line shows "Stopping…", the reconnecting message, "Playback progress is pending…", "Starting…", "Buffering", and "Loading subtitles…" before it, so "Subtitles are too large to show.", raised while file A's content buffered, expired unseen. Fix: a notice's clock (`playback.player.newNoticeClock`, `noticeShows`) counts only the time it shows: at each chrome refresh the time since the last refresh is spent only if nothing outranked it then (`busyText`, statusText without the notice), so a notice raised behind a status shows once that status ends, for its full 4 s, and one interrupted after it showed keeps what it had left. Refreshes come from the player's events (position reports while playing), as before, so a notice still clears at the first refresh after its time. Specs: the clock behind Buffering for 8 s then 4 s on screen; interrupted by Buffering and Loading subtitles…, then its remaining 3 s; the player (PlayerProbe): a selected sidecar's `too_large` while buffering keeps "Buffering" for 5 s, then shows the notice for 4 s once playing, and an unselected sidecar's failure says nothing. | AG-218 | Run 24 fix / run 24 observation 2 (AG-217 decision 1) | A subtitle-only `track_change` whose plan differs from the playing content only in the proxy node of its direct-play URL keeps the content (a retrack), a Roku deviation recorded in spec section 9.7. Server, re-read at silo-server `roku/client-support` `c166d16d1` and `origin/main` `5ec820486` (read only; the proxy code differs only in a bandwidth-probe route): the proxy's `handleDirectPlay` admits a request through `verifyPlaybackToken`, which checks the HS256 token's signature and `exp` (`streamtoken.Verify`, `jwt.ParseWithClaims`), the egress node frozen in that token's own claims against this node (`proxyEgressStatusV3`), and the session's deny marker (`sessionDenied`). The marker is written only when the session ends: a user or admin stop (`finishStopV2`, `endPlaybackSessionAuthority` when user-initiated, `markAttemptStoppedByAdmin`), an abort, or an expiry reap (`markAttemptStoppedServerSide`). A replan clears only the session's route fields and the planner's reservation (`playback_v3.go`: "A replan starts without an egress identity"; `planner.go` `PlanRoute`), neither of which the proxy reads, so the old node serves the old URL until its token's 24 h expire or the session stops. Rule (`playback.session.onOtherNode`): `retrackRefusals` names only `stream_origin`, and both plans' stream URLs are a node's direct-play route (absolute, ending in `/stream/direct/` once the token is removed) with the same query (`playback.media.otherNode`); the node's own path prefix, if any, is the node's. So it still reloads for `in_place` (a sidecar the content lacks or Roku does not list, every audio change: the player sends `inPlace` false), a quality change (never a retrack), another route (`/stream/remux/` and transcode tokens, whose job the server replaces, still count), another query, the configured origin on either side, any other media difference, and `url_expiry` or `credential_aging` (the kept URL within an hour of its plan's expiry). The retrack keeps the content's URL, `urlExpiresAt`, credential expiry, and renewal, as AG-208's did; the next comparison is against the newest plan, so a later plan on either node keeps it too. A node that goes away is a network failure the reconnect recovers from with a new plan (AG-217). Development builds log `kept the content on its proxy node` at debug level, with no fields. Specs: `otherNode` (two nodes, a node path prefix, the same node, the configured origin either way, remux, transcode, another route or query); the reducer keeps content, URL, and expiry across nodes; run 24 T2's sequences with alternating nodes (file A: Off, Off, Arabic, Off in place, English not carried reloads with `in_place` and `stream_origin`; file B: the same with German not carried) log no URL, host, or token; an audio change and a quality change whose plans move node reload; a kept URL 30 minutes from expiry reloads with `stream_origin`, `url_expiry`, `credential_aging`. Why 10 s: run 24's 8.3 s and a fifth more for a slower download or player; first extractions of embedded text (21.8 s in run 22, 26.1 s on run 24's stray episode, about 65 s on the reference file) are past it either way, and each waits 7 s more than before before its picture. Against the start-time target (8 s from the keypress, plan section 1; certification 3.6): file A cannot meet it on this TV whichever path it takes (Roku alone took 6.8–7.9 s from content to first frame on every load); the wait removes the late reload's stop and second start, about 10.4–11.1 s, when the sidecar comes within the cap, for an expected 15–16 s on file A's first start (not measured) and, with AG-220, about 7–8 s on later ones. While content waits for its selected sidecar, the player stops the content before it at once (`stopForSubtitle`, `playback.slot.halt`; info `the content waits for its subtitle; the Video node stops`): content played on during a 10 s wait would play again from the position the new plan starts at (an in-play pick replayed what played meanwhile; 3–4 s with the 3 s wait), and the start's own first content, stopped 8 s in, would be stopping in the window where run 24's slow stops fell (AG-221). The status line shows "Loading subtitles…" meanwhile, the controls stay up until the first frame, and the stall clock starts only when content is assigned (AG-159). Specs: `waitUntil` at 10 s for a pending selected sidecar (queued or loading) and 1 s once it is ready or failed; `selectedPending`; the player (PlayerProbe): a selected sidecar 8 s late arrives in the one load, the earlier content stopped at once and "Loading subtitles…" shown, no late reload; past the cap the content plays without it and the sidecar's arrival holds the late path's reload; unselected sidecars wait 1 s and leave the earlier content playing until then. Headless only | Device run of file A's first start of an app session and a pick of its ASS during play from Off (with the cache emptied by relaunching the app): one `content assigned` with the sidecar, no `the subtitle came after its content started`, no `still stopping`, "Loading subtitles…" until the picture, and the time from the plan to the first frame (expect about 15–16 s); a first extraction still plays at about 10 s and reloads once |
| AG-220 | Run 24 fix / run 24 observation 1 (AG-217 decision 3) | Cause: every start converted file A's sidecar again (16 of 16 attempts, about 8 s each). Server, read at `c166d16d1` and `origin/main` (read only): an inventory `track_id` is only a position (`file:{id}:subtitle:{ordinal}`, `TrackIDV3`), which a rescan (a sidecar added or removed), a re-probe, or a deleted download moves to another track; external and downloaded tracks (those with a `sync_key`) are retimed behind the same URL, by hand or by the automatic sync a first play starts by default (`subsync`, `Store.Apply`; the route sends `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` for that reason); embedded tracks change only with the file; nothing is per profile. So the cache keeps only a file's embedded text tracks (`source` `embedded`, no `sync_key`), keyed (`playback.sidecars.cacheKey`) by the plan's file (`effective_media_file_id` equal to `source.media_file_id`), the entry's `track_id`, codec, language, label, and flags (so a rescan that puts another track at that position misses), the format served, and the timeline offset the cues were moved by; never the URL, which names the session. `playback.sidecarCache`: the index lives in `m.global` (the player reads and writes it on the render thread); MediaTask writes such a conversion to `tmp:/sidecar-cache/sidecar<n>.srt` (no path contains another) and reports its size; the player keeps every such result, a replaced plan's too, and offers a kept file as ready, with no download and no "Loading subtitles…". Bound: 4 MiB (`MAX_BYTES`) and 32 files: under 1% of the 484–519 MB free in run 24, a tenth of the 36–43 MB the app used on Detail, room for file A's four tracks (about 1.4 MB) or two at the 20,000-cue limit; tmp:/ counts as the app's memory and outlives the player. The least recently used go first, never a file the Video node's content or the current load's sidecars name, or a conversion still to be written; an entry whose file is gone is dropped (`roFileSystem.Exists`), and a file no entry names is deleted when the cache is trimmed (after each conversion kept, and when the player closes); at Roku's memory level pressure or critical, while a player is open, it keeps only what plays. Not covered: a file replaced on the server under the same ID during the session; an aborted chunked transfer that Roku reported as complete would be kept (Roku documents a failed transfer as a negative code; not checked). Development builds log `subtitle reused from this session` (format, selected). Specs: keys for an embedded track (no URL or session in them), none for external or downloaded tracks or a plan whose file IDs differ, other keys for another file, offset, format, or description; the index's take, missing files, least-recently-used eviction with pins, the bound, a replaced key and a stray file; the player: a file's second start finds its two embedded tracks ready and downloads only the external one, a pick of the other embedded track reuses it, another file converts, a deleted file converts again, and the cache stays within 4 MiB. Headless only | Device run: file A's second start in an app session logs `subtitle reused from this session` for its ASS, no `api: download budget` for it, no `media: subtitle converted` for it, and its first frame about 7–8 s after the plan; after relaunching the app it converts again |
| AG-221 | Run 24 fix / run 24 observation 1 (AG-217 decision 4) | Conversion (MediaTask, `playback.subtitles`): a synthetic 9.94 MB ASS shaped like file A's (8,250 drawing events of about 1.2 KB with long override blocks, 330 tags-only events, 330 signs over two frames, 330 dialogue lines), converted under brs-node 2.6.1, took 4.9 s before and 1.8–2.0 s after (660 cues, 8,580 dropped, 330 merged, the same SRT by MD5); a 635 KB SRT of 8,723 cues (the shape of file A's other two tracks, which took 3.3–3.6 s each on the TV) 3.9 s before and 3.1 s after. The benchmark is a local harness, not committed; brs-node's 4.9 s compares with the TV's 5.3 s for the 10.24 MiB file, so the TV is expected near 2 s (not measured). Where the time went: reading and splitting 0.8 s; the rest was per Dialogue line: a trimmed copy of each line, nine copies of its remainder to split its fields, and both times parsed digit by digit before the text showed it a drawing (92% of file A's events are dropped). Changes: a line is copied only when it starts with a space; the fields are walked by position and only the times and text are cut out; the text is read first and the times only for events with text; Format's time positions are found once; the times are matched by one pattern a reader makes once (`timePattern`, `^([0-9]+):([0-9]+)(?::([0-9]+))?(?:[,.]([0-9]+))?$`), as the digit-by-digit reading accepted them; the SRT and WebVTT tag patterns are made once per reader, not per cue; substring checks skip the drawing and override patterns for text without `{` or `\p`, the line-break pattern for text without `\`, and the carriage-return copy for a chunk without one. Changed edge: an ASS Format whose Text is not last (invalid ASS) now drops its events. Slow stop: the 3.3–3.6 s stops were all four late reloads of file A's starts, each 0.43–0.72 s after Roku listed the MKV's own tracks (Roku had opened the file) and before the first frame; the two late reloads after picks stopped in 158–163 ms, before that listing, and the stray episode's, after its first frame, in 775 ms. A 652 KB SRT conversion started within 0.15 s of all seven, so the conversion does not explain it, and the 3 s "still stopping" warning fired on time, so the render thread was not starved. Inferred, not proven: Roku's stop is slow while it starts an MKV it has opened (a 4K HEVC original through a proxy, resuming mid-file). Not changed beyond AG-219, which stops the start's first content within about 0.2 s of its assignment (expected, not measured), long before Roku opens it, and avoids the late reload when the sidecar comes within the cap. Specs: times matched as before (13 cases with and without a reader's pattern, hours past 99), Format with the times elsewhere and commas in the text, Text not last, and indented rows; the existing ASS, SRT, WebVTT, chunk, and typeset specs unchanged. Headless only | Device run: `media: subtitle converted` for file A's ASS about 2–3 s after its download ends (5.3 s in run 24), and its 652 KB SRTs about 2.8 s; a late reload's stop, if any, logged with when Roku listed the file's tracks, to confirm or refute the inference |
| AG-222 | Owner request 2026-10-23 ("make sure our page layout matches tvOS"; "version/audio/subtitle selectors on the detail pages") and the tvOS parity audit (silo-apple `b90a86ea`, silo-android `0545b85de`; coordinator decisions D0–D12) | The audit found 32 P1, 80 P2 and 90 P3 gaps; most come from spec 5.1's rule that Roku follows Android TV where the apps differ, not from tvOS drift since `5c1dcfb0`. Decisions: (D0) tvOS is the primary reference for layout, sizes, type, copy and structure; Android TV only where tvOS is silent or where Roku shares its constraints (self-drawn focus, no blur, platform keyboard), each such value recorded in spec 5.9; (D1) detail playback selection as tvOS ships it: three always-mounted circle buttons in the action row (Versions, Audio Tracks, Subtitles; disabled, not hidden, when there is no choice) and the passive VERSION · AUDIO · SUBTITLES readout above the row; no separate selector row and no Edition control (tvOS deleted both in `c2a64022`); Versions moves from spec 3.3 into the first release at the owner's request, and a pinned version sends `allow_alternate_versions: false` as tvOS does; (D2) audio and subtitle picks are saved to the server as tvOS saves them (`/api/v2/{audio,subtitle}-prefs/{series or content}`), and Auto audio honours `effective_audio_track_index`; this replaces spec 8.5's "the client writes no settings" for these two; (D3) Search is a pushed route without the top bar; (D4) cards lift on focus as tvOS's do (about ×1.14 posters, ×1.2 landscape, with a shadow 9-patch and no ring); the ring returns only if a device check shows focus is unclear; (D5) launch keeps reopening the last profile (no first-release setting could undo tvOS's "Open automatically?"), with 5.4 and 5.9 reworded so they no longer cite tvOS as the model; (D6) first-run forms move to the 900 px column and the card carries Roku help (the remote, or the Roku mobile app's keyboard); message screens lose the card; (D7) Search uses tvOS's centred 760 px segmented control; (D8) after a finished episode, focus goes to the next episode in play order, played or not, else the next regular season's first episode, never into Specials (tvOS); (D9) the player chrome is rebuilt to tvOS (66 px icon buttons, gradient, title block, 1100×380 Options HUD with tabs above and column panes, Quality as a row that opens a picker, controls hidden under the HUD), keeping Roku's caption-mode column (certification); (D10) into the first release: the hero credit line, external ratings, quality badges, `ui.title_art`, and Skip Recap with recap auto-skip; deferred to 3.3: the buffering-quality pill and prompts, spoiler hiding, the AI-translation marker, theme music, and Home's per-card hero detail line (an item read per rested card); (D11) Up Next stays at the credits, with 5.9's wording fixed; (D12) Home's hero text block ends at y 536 (the repo's tvOS screenshot), to be checked against a tvOS frame when one can be captured (the simulator refused the lab's plain-HTTP address). Work: a foundation package lands Appendix A's tokens and Part IV's spec text with these decisions; then WP1 shell/Home/cards, WP3 libraries/Search, WP4 player, WP5 first-run/profiles/PIN/states in parallel; the selector package (WP-S) now, and WP2 detail layout after WP-S | Audit; owner request |
| AG-223 | Detail selectors (the owner: "the version/audio/subtitle selectors on the detail pages") | **Assumed.** The reference is what tvOS shipped at silo-apple `origin/main` `b90a86ea` (`TVPlaybackSelectorRow.swift`, `TVDetailActions.swift`, `TVDetailHero.swift`, `TVMovieDetailView.swift`, `TVSeriesDetailView.swift`, `TVItemDetailView.swift`), not its 2026-06-15 design spec: no Edition selector and no squared selector row, but Versions, Audio Tracks, and Subtitles circles after Play and Start Over, and a passive VERSION, AUDIO, SUBTITLES readout above the row, on movies and on a series' active episode. Visibility follows that code: the three are always there, disabled until the item's watch state is read; Versions opens with any version (Auto plus that version when there is one), Audio Tracks with any audio track, Subtitles always (Auto may play none). The design's rules (Edition with two editions, Version with two versions, Audio with two tracks) are not what shipped. They replace Detail's Audio and Subtitles circles (spec section 5.6) | tvOS ships the design's selector row or an Edition selector |

Writes of one kind go one at a time under an owner the page never releases, only the latest waiting choice is kept, a waiting one is sent when the page is released, and a failure is logged without the response and not retried. The version is page state, as tvOS's `preferredVersionFileId` is; the server's `last_file_id` is Auto's next time. Spec section 8.5's "the client does not write settings" stands: these are not settings. It shows as the viewer's choice, where tvOS shows the remembered track as Auto's. | AG-236 | Run 25 fix / run 25 defect 1 (AG-220) | Cause: `playback.sidecarCache.exists` created `roFileSystem` in the player, on the render thread. Roku refuses it there ("creating MAIN\|TASK-only component failed on RENDER thread"), intermittently, and the app stopped four times in five sessions: twice when a conversion was kept (`cacheSidecar` → `trimCache` → `evict`) and twice on the player's release (`onRelease` → `trimCache`). brs-node does not enforce the rule. Fix: the player keeps only the index on the render thread (`playback.sidecarCache`: keys, paths, sizes, least-recently-used order; `take`, `put`, `forget`, and `trim` touch no file), and MediaTask does every file operation (`playback.sidecarFiles`, which only MediaTask imports). `{ id, track, kept }` asks whether a kept file is there; `{ keep }` deletes the files in `tmp:/sidecar-cache` that the trimmed index and the pins do not name (the least recently used past the bound, a replaced key's file once nothing pins it, and strays). A kept track is offered as ready at once, as before (no wait, no "Loading subtitles…"), and MediaTask is asked about its file. One found gone leaves the index and downloads and converts again: content not built yet waits for it as for any download, and built content takes a selected one through the late path's reload (AG-201). Development builds log `the subtitle kept from this session is gone; it downloads again`. The trim after a kept conversion and at memory pressure goes to MediaTask (started if needed). At release it goes before MediaTask's `finish`, and only if the player started MediaTask; a player that never did touched no cache file. Unchanged: 4 MiB and 32 files, embedded tracks only, keyed by the server's description and never the URL, least recently used first with pins. Not covered: a sweep sent before a replaced plan's conversion was kept can delete that file, which is then found gone when next offered and converts again; and a release whose MediaTask was not ready yet loses its sweep, so those files wait for the next player's first sweep. Specs: the index gives back a kept file without looking for it; forget; least-recently-used eviction with pins and the keep list; MAX_BYTES and MAX_ENTRIES; a replaced key. MediaTask's answers for a file that is there and one that is gone, and its sweep of the least recently used, a replaced file once unpinned, and a stray. The player (PlayerProbe with a FakeMediaTask that answers at once): reuse, another file converts, a kept file found gone downloads again with "Loading subtitles…", and the bound. Headless only | Device run as run 25's T2 and T7: file A's start, then Back and Resume twice in one app session (`subtitle reused from this session`, no ASS download or conversion); 10 × Home → Continue Watching → Detail → Resume 20 s → Back → Back; then a relaunch. Expect no `MAIN\|TASK-only` error, no `EXIT_BRIGHTSCRIPT_CRASH` at the next launch, and Detail back after each Back |
| AG-237 | Run 25 fix (defect 1's class) | `scripts/check-thread-rules.js` (`npm run check:thread-rules`, part of `npm run ci`) fails when a script in a render-thread scope creates a component Roku allows only in a Task node or on the main thread. A render-thread scope is any component under `components/` that does not extend Task (directly or through another component): its XML's scripts (inline ones included), its ancestors' scripts, and their imports, transitively. Every `#if` branch is checked, names match in any case, and comments and strings are ignored. The check works per file: a file any render-thread scope imports counts whether or not that function runs there, so Task-only code lives in files only Tasks import (as `playback.sidecarFiles` does). The list comes from Roku's "BrightScript support" page (developer.roku.com/docs/developer-program/core-concepts/scenegraph-brightscript/brightscript-support.md), which "Render thread restrictions" (core-concepts/threads.md) names as the per-thread list. "Can only be used in a Task node": roDatagramSocket, roFileSystem, roSocketAddress, roStreamSocket, roUrlTransfer. "Can only be used in a Task node or in the main BrightScript thread": roChannelStore. The pages for roFileSystem, roUrlTransfer, and roChannelStore state no thread themselves. Left out: the page's "Should only be used in a Task node" rows (ParseJson, ParseXml, ReadAsciiFile, roAudioMetadata, roImageMetadata), which are advice rather than a refusal; its rows for components that "cannot be used in a SceneGraph application" (roAppManager among them), a different rule, and this code uses none of them; and roHttpAgent and roDeviceInfo, which have no row and whose pages state no thread. The global file functions (`DeleteFile`, `ListDir`, `WriteAsciiFile`, `CreateDirectory`) are on no list, so the player's `DeleteFile` of a download for a replaced plan (`onSubtitleDownloaded`) stays. No exceptions. On the tree at `c07e7ae` its only hit was this defect's `source/playback/sidecarCache.bs`; after the fix, none. Tests: a fixture tree where a render-thread screen imports a source file that creates roFileSystem (and, through another import, roUrlTransfer under `#if`), a component XML's inline script creates roStreamSocket, and Tasks, direct and inherited, create them all; the command line exits 1 on it | Roku documents another component as Task-only, or a device refuses one this list allows |
| AG-238 | Run 25 observation 1 (T2) | Not changed. Reused starts took 8.3–9.5 s from the plan to the first frame against AG-220's expected 7–8 s. From the console's timings of the five reused starts (two in T2, two in T7, and an in-play pick of the ASS): Auto's `track_change` plan 0.16–0.17 s after the start's plan, the reuse logged with it, and content assigned 1.30–1.35 s after the plan (the 1 s `OTHERS_GRACE_MS` for the unselected sidecars, then a 126–141 ms stop of the start's first content; the in-play pick 1.76 s, with a 752 ms stop). Roku's own content-to-first-frame time (`VODStartComplete`) was 6.87–8.04 s, against 7.01–7.16 s on the run's three first starts that converted the ASS. So the client's share of a reused start is a steady 1.3 s; the rest, and its spread, is Roku starting the stream at different positions from different proxy nodes, and with five and three samples the console shows no client cause. AG-220's estimate used run 24's 6.8–7.9 s for Roku and left out the grace. MediaTask converts the other sidecars during Roku's start on both kinds of start. The only client lever is the 1 s grace: it bought the content the 26 KB external SRT (ready 0.2 s after the reuse; the two 652 KB ones take about 3 s each and never make it). Dropping it when the selected track is reused would save about 1 s but send the others to a reload when picked, a product trade-off (AG-219) not made here | A device run that times Roku's content-to-first-frame with and without conversions running, or the owner rules on the grace |

| AG-286 | W12 UI review, major 1 (preference writes race on leaving detail) | Cause: the page's release sent its waiting write (`flushPreferences`) while the one before it was still on its way, and the scheduler runs several interactive transfers at once, so a Japanese `PUT` and the Auto `DELETE` after it could reach the server in either order. Chosen: one line per kind and key, sent in order, rather than a sequence number with stale writes dropped. The server's `PUT` and `DELETE /api/v2/{audio,subtitle}-prefs/{key}` take no `If-Match` or version (silo-server `origin/main` `88d86ba4`, `contracts/api/v2/openapi.json`), so once two writes are in flight nothing stops the older landing last; only holding a key's next write until the last one's result keeps the latest. The line lives in AppScene's router (`api.router.order`, a request's `orderKey`), which outlives every screen: requests with one key go to ApiTask one at a time, in the order sent, each after the result of the one before; a write waiting last gives way to a newer write (its handle gets `cancelled`, reason `superseded`), so a burst of picks sends the one in flight and the latest; a read is never replaced and waits its turn. A request that may wait is fenced to the identity it was sent under (`fence`, as playback jobs are), so a write waiting through a profile switch fails `identity_changed` rather than landing on the other profile, as tvOS captures the owner at dispatch. The detail page sends each changed choice at once, its `orderKey` its path (`catalog.request.savePreference`); its own chain and its release flush (AG-224) are gone, and a reopened page's writes join the same line. tvOS (`TrackSelectionPersistence.WriteOrder`, `b90a86ea`) chains every write per kind and key without replacing waiting ones; the end state is the same. Not covered: a write that times out (15 s) is aborted and the next one goes, and the server may still apply the aborted one later; a reused request ID (only a bug) would hold its line. Specs: the router's lines, replaying the review (Japanese sent, Auto waiting, the page released, a reopened page's English replacing Auto and going only after Japanese's result), keys apart, a read behind a write, cancel and owner release of a waiting request; the page sends each change at once with its line key; the request builders' keys. Headless only | Device run: on a slow server, Audio Tracks → Japanese, then Auto, then Back at once; reopening the title shows Auto, and tvOS starts it on Auto |
| AG-287 | W12 UI review, major 2 (a late audio preference read overwrites a newer choice) | Cause: `onAudioPreference` replaced the page's remembered audio whenever it answered, and `m.audioPicked` guarded only the current episode, so a read answering after the viewer chose Japanese carried the older English to the next episode's start. Fix: an audio pick that changes the choice cancels the read still on its way (`cancelKind("audioPreference")`, which takes it out of its line or cancels it in ApiTask), and from then on the pick is the page's remembered track; `m.audioPicked` is gone. The read also waits in its key's line behind writes an earlier page sent (`catalog.request.audioPreference`'s `orderKey`), so a page reopened while its last pick is on its way reads that pick. tvOS reads no audio preference (the server folds it into `effective_audio_track_index`), so it has no such race; the read is Roku's (AG-227). Specs: the review's scenario on a series page (the read pending, Japanese chosen, the read's English answer finding it cancelled, then the next episode: Play names Japanese, and nothing is read again); a read that answers after a subtitle pick still seeds. Headless only | AG-227's revisit (Roku's Auto stops naming audio), which removes the read |
| AG-288 | W12 UI review, minor (confirming an unchanged subtitle Off writes a preference) | A Roku deviation (spec section 5.9). OK on the value a selector already shows writes nothing, for Audio Tracks and Subtitles alike; moving to Auto, Off, or a track still writes. tvOS at `b90a86ea` does write here: `TVActionPopoverMenu`'s rows call `onSelect` whatever is selected, and `persistSubtitleSelection` with -1 sends `subtitleOffRequest`, so a seeded Off (`serverPreferredSubtitleIndex`: mode off without forced subtitles) becomes the title's own preference. The brief asked for a write only on a change; the reason is that the page seeds Off from the profile's settings, and a title preference written by a press that changed nothing would keep the title Off after the profile's subtitle setting changes. Specs: a seeded Off accepted again, and Auto audio and Auto subtitles accepted again, write nothing; the same track twice writes once; moving to Auto or Off writes | tvOS stops writing an unchanged pick (the row then goes), or the owner prefers tvOS's behaviour |
| AG-289 | W12 playback review, major 1 (the subtitle cache reuses another server's file) | Cause: `playback.sidecars.cacheKey` named the file by its ID alone, IDs are each server's, and the index in `m.global` outlives a server change (Profiles → Change server resets the navigation, not the app). Fix: the key's first member is the configured origin (`api.session.current().origin`, the base `offerSidecars` already resolves against), so a lookup, a new entry, and a conversion still in flight (`cacheResult` keeps the key its request carried) all stay in their server's part; and AppScene empties the index whenever ApiTask publishes a session that is not signed in (`forgetSidecars`), since a later sign-in can reach another server at the same address (account IDs are sequential, "1", so they cannot tell). The next player's trim sweeps the forgotten files (AG-236), so they wait in `tmp:/` until a player opens or the app exits. Not per profile: a kept file is a file's embedded text track converted to SRT at the plan's offset, the same for every profile that may play the file (AG-220), and a profile that may not never gets a plan naming it. The key holds the origin but no path, session, token, or query. The render thread still keeps only the index and MediaTask does every file operation (AG-236); `npm run check:thread-rules` passes. Specs: one plan's key differs under two origins, and server A's kept file is not server B's; the player (PlayerProbe) converts file 42 again on server B after keeping server A's, and still reuses A's. Not covered headless: AppScene's emptying at sign-out (AppScene is not built headless) | Device run: play a file with an embedded subtitle on server A, Back, Profiles → Change server to server B, play a file with the same ID: no `subtitle reused from this session`, and its own cues; after signing out and in, the next start converts again |
| AG-290 | W12 playback review, major 2 (a rescan gives an embedded track another's cached cues) | Cause: the key named a track by its inventory `track_id` (a combined position, `TrackIDV3`) and its description, and two embedded tracks can describe themselves alike (two untitled English ASS tracks), so an external sidecar added by a rescan moved A to B's old position and key. Fix: an embedded track is named by its stream in the file, the `embedded_stream_index` the server puts on every embedded sidecar URL beside `file_id` (silo-server `origin/main` `88d86ba4`: `ScopeSubtitleInventoryV3` adds it from `file.SubtitleTracks[ordinal].Index`, and the subtitle route resolves the track by it "independently of its combined ordinal"). The client reads those two query values (`playback.sidecars.embeddedStream`), keeps the stream only when `file_id` is the plan's file and the value is a whole number, and never keeps the URL. The key is now origin, file, stream, codec, language, label, forced, default, hearing impaired, format, and offset; `track_id` (AG-220) left it, so a track keeps its conversion across a rescan, and a URL naming no stream keeps nothing (no reuse rather than a guess). The v3 inventory item has no stream field or server track ID of its own (`SubtitleInventoryItemV3`). Specs: the review's inventories (A in stream 3 and B in stream 4, then an external track first): A's new key misses B's file and B's finds its own; no key without the stream, with another file's `file_id`, or with an index that is not a number | The server adds a stable track identity to the inventory item, or stops putting `embedded_stream_index` on sidecar URLs |
| AG-291 | W12 playback review, minor (Back exits playback while Skip Recap shows) | **Not changed: Roku already matches tvOS.** The brief said to match tvOS. tvOS at `b90a86ea` (and `origin/main` `79a3de25`): `PlayerView`'s `onExitCommand` dismisses only the buffering pill and the intro pill (`dismissIntroSkipPrompt`); with the segment pill (Skip Recap or Skip Credits) showing it hides the controls while playing, and otherwise dismisses the player; `segmentSkipLayer` has no exit handler, and the scrubber's `onExitWhenIdle` passes over it too. | AG-292 | Device run 26 (coordinator decisions) | Run 26 (`c3d474f`, the owner's server) found the rebuilt UI within a few px of spec section 5 almost everywhere, the run 25 crash gone (convert, reuse, relaunch), the detail selections reaching the player, the subtitle regression checks passing, detail opening in 0.9–2.1 s, rows scrolling at a median 53.5 fps, and 10 of 10 stability cycles with flat memory. Defects: subtitles draw over the shown player controls; the Quality picker sits over the Options tabs; Options Info cuts its overview's last line and lists end on half rows; a long detail Subtitles menu covers its own button and the readout; a focused card shows a white line on its right edge; after choosing Auto the readout keeps naming the track just left. Comparing the screenshots with tvOS's `home.jpg` and `movie-detail.jpg`, the coordinator also finds three differences worth closing under D0: the page behind the hero is black where tvOS washes it with the backdrop's colour; the detail readout sits in fixed columns with wide gaps where tvOS flows it inline; and the detail meta line starts with the rating as plain text where tvOS ends it with the rating in a chip (Home's hero already draws the chip). Decisions: (1) fix the six defects (player: lift subtitles above the shown controls or hide them under the controls as tvOS does, keep the Quality picker inside the HUD, whole rows and lines only in Options panes; detail: the Subtitles menu keeps its button and the readout visible, the readout follows the selection at once; cards: no edge line); (2) a base wash from the backdrop's `backdrop_thumbhash` average colour, darkened as tvOS darkens it, fills the hero region of Home and detail and fades to black above the first row of cards, so the 9-patch card corners still sit on black (AG-253's reason holds below the fade, not above it); (3) the detail readout flows inline with tvOS's gaps, measuring each value's width; (4) the detail meta line follows tvOS's order with the rating in the same chip as Home's hero; (5) the dev build logs the start body's field names (never values) at debug level so device runs can confirm the hand-off | Run 26 fixes |
| AG-293 | Run 26 defect 1 (AG-292 (1)): captions over the shown controls | tvOS at `b90a86ea` neither raises nor hides its subtitles: `PlayerSubtitleLayer` sits on the video (`PlayerView.swift` 749–755), its cues about 86 pt above the video's foot (`AetherSubtitleOverlay.swift` 339–347), and `TVPlayerControls` draws over them with its 240 pt gradient, so the controls cover a cue. Roku draws captions above the UI, so the client hides them instead: `Player.drawCaptions` sets `Video.suppressCaptions` while `ControlsOverlay` shows (not while Options is open, which leaves the foot clear, nor under Up Next) and clears it as they hide, keeping `playback.sidecars.suppressed`'s value apart (`m.subtitleSuppressed`). Roku documents the field as the node's own, for "resolving conflicts in cases where UI elements are drawn" (Video node page, read 2026-10-24); `globalCaptionMode`, `subtitleTrack`, the sidecars, and the selection, settle, and confirm logic (AG-190, AG-193 to AG-207) are untouched. Content within `SUBTITLE_SETTLE_MS` of its first frame is not written for it (hardware run 20's stops followed writes then), and a write counts as the player's (`noteWrite`), so a stop right after one reloads at the position. Consequence: no caption shows while paused with the controls up (on tvOS the controls and gradient cover it). Not chosen: `Video.captionRenderArea` (Roku OS 15.3 and later) can draw captions in an `override` box and so lift them above the title block; it is unexercised here, a lifted cue would meet the 340 px preview card and the raised Skip pills, and older Roku OS versions lack it.
