| AG-301 | Run 26 fixes (AG-292 (4)): the detail meta line | **Assumed:** tvOS's order is its `b90a86ea` code, not the older screenshot: `TVDetailHero.factsRow` leads with the rating chip (since `8919039c`, after `movie-detail.jpg` at `61c2400e` showed it last), then the facts, the ratings, and the genres, 14 px apart with a 45% dot between texts (none after the chip). Roku keeps that order and draws the rating in Home's hero chip (`theme.styleChip` and `theme.placeChip`, shared with `Hero`), the items and dots as Labels laid out by measured width; the genres (or "Season N · Episode M") give way first with an ellipsis and go below 80 px, and an item that does not fit goes with the rest, so ratings drop from the end, as tvOS's `ViewThatFits` drops them. `catalog.detail.metadata` and `episodeLine` now give `{ rating, items, tail }`, `episodeFacts` a list; a route's seed carries the card's rating for the chip, ahead of the card's own meta line | Device screenshots of a movie, a series, and a focused episode's line; tvOS moving the chip again |
| AG-302 | Run 26 fixes (AG-292 (2)): the hero wash | The base wash: `util.thumbhash.averageColor` reads a ThumbHash's DC terms (the reference `thumbHashToAverageRGBA`), and `catalog.art.washColor` moves the colour's luminance to 0.22 as tvOS's `HeroBackdropPalette.normalize` does (a dark one lifted at most 4.4 times) and paints it at 42% over black (`TVDetailPageSurface`), opaque; the map step does this, so cards (`CardContent.tint`) and detail items carry the colour. silo-server `origin/main` sends `backdrop_thumbhash` on `CatalogItem` (Home and library rows) and `CatalogItemDetail`; the poster's hash is used where the poster stands in for a missing backdrop. **Assumed:** 42% on Home too (tvOS's Home gradient runs from 100% at the top right to 18% at the bottom left), the wash full to y 287 (the backdrop mask's first stop) and fading linearly to black at y 596 on Home and libraries and at y 690 on detail; the backdrop's fades are redrawn grey-coded with the wash's weight per row and tinted with its colour (`hero_fade_*`, `detail_fade_*`, `wash_fade.png` from `scripts/ui-art.py`), so without a tint they are the black fades they replace. One Rectangle and one stretched 2×256 Poster per hero, hidden without a tint; on Home four `ColorFieldInterpolator`s in the backdrop's cross-fade carry the colour, the first card's at once; on detail it is set at once | Device screenshots of Home and detail with a warm and a dark backdrop, and a frame-rate check of the Home cross-fade on a 512 MB device |
| AG-303 | Run 27 defect 1: the tick under the lifted first card | Read from run 27's Home screenshot: the tick is column x 43, rows 890–893, in the progress bar's colour and rows, while the lifted card's art and frame start at x 44. The cause is float rounding: a card scaled 1.2 about its centre (180, 100) has its left edge at 80 + 180 − 180 × 1.2, and BrightScript's single-precision 1.2 (1.2000000477) makes that 43.99998; at x 444 and beyond the sum rounds to the whole px, so only the first card showed it. Roku drew the bar, a `Rectangle`, from x 43, past the 9-patch frame's corner, while the art and frame `Poster`s drew nothing there. **Assumed** from the same shot: Roku truncates a scaled `Rectangle`'s position and size (the lifted bar, 4.8 px from y 890.2, draws rows 890–893). Fix: `theme.browse.liftSides` gives each side's whole-px growth; `CatalogCard` scales the card about its top-left corner and moves it back by that lift times the focus, so at rest and when lifted the card's left and top edges are its translation, whole px (a Continue Watching card 432×240 from (−36, room − 20) in its item; posters −13, −19). Specs: the lifted landscape card's translation and parts' edges, and `liftSides` per shape. Not changed: the lifted bar's 4.8 px still draws 4 rows, leaving the art's last row (y 894) under it on every lifted landscape card, as in runs 26 and 27; it is 1 px, was not reported, and a fix would depend on the assumed truncation | Device screenshot of the lifted first Continue Watching card on an FHD Roku (x 43 dark) and an HD Roku, whose 2/3 UI scale makes edges fractional again |
| AG-304 | Run 27 observation (1): no Quality row checked while the row read Auto | Auto is checked whenever the session's quality preference is auto (`playback.options.currentQuality`; a spec covers it), so run 27's picker, with no row checked, ran under a saved resolution: the profile's `playback.preferred_quality` (480p, 720p, 1080p, or 2160p), with the original playing (`validated_original_playback`) and no rung matching. **Inferred** from the code: the run did not read the setting and the console does not log it. tvOS `b90a86ea` (`ApplePlaybackQuality.highlightedOptionId`) checks no row then too, and its Quality row names the preference (`TVPlayerInfoHUD` `qualityValue`: "Up to 1080p HD"); Roku's row fell back to "Auto", which read as Auto chosen but unchecked. Fix: `playback.options.qualityValue` names the preference when no row is checked (`preferenceName`: 2160p "4K", 1080p and 720p "Up to … HD", 480p "Up to 480p", as tvOS's `displayName`; a rung chosen in Options that the plan no longer lists by its label, where tvOS shows its local catalog's name or Auto), so the row reads Auto only while Auto is checked; spec 5.7 and 9.7. Not changed: without a reported rung, Roku still checks a row whose label is the preference itself (tvOS lights only the viewer's own pick) | Device screenshots of the picker under an Auto and a saved 1080p preference |
| AG-305 | Run 27 observation (2): the player's and the detail page's subtitle lists disagree | Traced: the detail menu lists the watch projection's version `subtitle_tracks` (`catalog.selection` → `catalog.pickers.subtitleOptions`) in tvOS's `SubtitleDisplayOrder`, led by the projection's `effective_subtitle_language`; Options listed the plan's `subtitle.inventory` in the server's combined order (sidecars by file, then embedded) and named rows by Android TV's `meaningfulSubtitleRowTitle`, which hid bracketed or long titles the detail menu shows. tvOS `b90a86ea`: its player lists the plan's inventory too (`ApplePlaybackV3PlanAdapter.subtitlePickerTracks`: "V3 inventory is an authoritative menu"), ordered by `SubtitleDisplayOrder` with `watchDetail.effectiveSubtitleLanguage` and named with `TrackLabels`, as its detail page names the version's tracks. Fix: Options orders the inventory with `catalog.pickers.subtitleOrder`, led by `playback.options.subtitleOrderLanguage` (the projection's effective language, which `catalog.selection` now reads through the same function), and names it with `catalog.pickers.subtitleTitle` on the inventory label, Forced and SDH by flag or title. The file-name rule from Android TV (a title ending `.srt` and the like) moved into `catalog.pickers.subtitleTitle`, so both lists drop the file name the server gives an untitled sidecar as its title and its label; `playback.options.namedLabel`, `languageName`, and `subtitleCodecName` went with it. A spec builds both lists from one file's tracks and checks the same names, details, and order. Each side keeps its source, as on tvOS: silo-server `origin/main` writes both from the same file record (`buildVersionSubtitleTracks`, `BuildSubtitleInventoryV3`), so the Spanish sidecar run 27 saw only in Options came from the owner's server's projection lacking it (cause not seen: the console does not log bodies); a detail pick is an ordinal into the projection's list (`subtitle_track_index`), so the detail menu cannot offer it. The detail line's flags keep each tvOS surface's order (Options: format, Default, Forced, SDH; detail: format, Forced, SDH, Default). Run 27's "pinning the version moves the chosen track up the detail menu" was the language key: the German pick's `PUT subtitle-prefs` made German the series' effective language, which the re-read projection reported; tvOS's detail leads with the profile's own subtitle language instead (spec 5.9). Selection and settle (AG-193 to AG-221) are unchanged: rows carry the same `track_id` values and checks | Device screenshots of both lists on a file with several sidecars, and of a sidecar added after the scan |
| AG-306 | Device runs 26–28 (coordinator decision on the stability bound) | Runs 27 and 28 kept the node count flat over 10 cycles (1,203→1,206; 1,206→1,206) with no BrightScript error, while `chanperf` memory moved +11% (run 27, flat from cycle 1, measured soon after a relaunch) and −8.3% (run 28, after a warm-up cycle), single readings ranging about ±6% with no trend. Decision: a stability pass needs the node count within 5% and no upward trend in memory across the cycles (a least-squares slope under 0.2 MB per cycle over at least 10 cycles, after one warm-up cycle), not two `chanperf` endpoints within 5%, which the device's own noise exceeds; a single reading 15% over the warm baseline still fails | Release device runs |
| AG-307 | Cleanup: the headless suite in shards | brs-node keeps every component a process creates until it exits (60 discarded PlayerProbes failed under a 600 MB heap, about 23 MB each), so one process for the suite peaked at 5.3 GB RSS and needed the 8 GB heap override. `npm test` (`scripts/test-headless.sh`, which now runs `scripts/test-headless.py`) builds one test package per shard from `bsconfig.tests.json` with only the shard's specs, and runs each in its own brs-node process, `TEST_JOBS` at a time (default 4, at most the CPU count). Shards are a stable list (`SHARDS`): directories or spec files, a spec going to the longest path that holds it; the run fails when a spec has no shard, a path is listed twice, or a path holds no spec. A shard fails when the build fails, brs-cli exits non-zero or outlives the 5-minute timeout (terminated, then killed 10 s later), Rooibos reports FAIL or no PASS, or no test ran (a misplaced spec otherwise passes with 0 tests). `--junit` merges the shards' reports, the first's properties and every `testsuite` in shard order, into one `reports/junit.xml`, written whenever every shard produced a report, failures included, as before (`scripts/update-goldens.sh`). Eight shards; peak RSS alone: catalog 1.13 GB, playback 1.33, player-captions 1.61, player-sidecars 1.76, detail 1.42, detail-tracks 1.61, views 1.56, screens 0.95; each also passes under a 2 GB heap cap (an 8 GB machine's default), so the 8 GB override is gone. On 4 CPUs (`taskset`, as GitHub's runner): 50.0 s before, 31.1 s after with 17 more tests; 22.7 s on this 32-core host; 69 s with `TEST_JOBS=1`. Python 3 is now needed for `npm test` itself (README). Not measured on GitHub Actions. Later, the views shard (`tests/views` and most of `tests/player`) peaked at 3.2 GB RSS under this host's 4 GB default heap and died of JS heap exhaustion on GitHub's 7 GB runner, whose default is about 2 GB; `tests/player` became a ninth shard (views 2.34 GB RSS under a 2 GB heap, the rest at most 1.86; all pass at 1536 MB, views and player-sidecars fail at 1280), and every shard now runs with a 2 GB heap (`HEAP_MB`) so a shard that would fail on CI fails here too. A crashed or timed-out shard names the cause and the case it was in, and on GitHub Actions adds an error annotation, since `gh run view --log` can cut the output before the summary. `tests/views/detailPersonal.spec.bs` runs in a tenth shard, detail-personal: in views, and later in player, screens, and player-captions, it made that shard die of heap exhaustion at 1536 MB (player already did so on 54a7568). `tests/views/detailExtras.spec.bs` runs in the catalog shard: in views it left that shard failing at 1280 MB, and in catalog both shards pass at 1024 | A shard's peak passes 2.5 GB, a CI run slower than before, or a brs-node release that frees discarded components |

The specs now import the theme files they use, and the helpers moved to `tests/helpers/` (`playback.bs`, `harness.bs`, `serverContract.bs`; `tests/dev/generate_server_contract.py` writes the new path); every spec builds alone. `tests/player/captions.spec.bs` (62 PlayerProbe tests, about 2.1 GB of heap) and `tests/views/detailScreen.spec.bs` (40 DetailScreenProbe tests, about 1.8 GB) are split at a describe block into `captionSidecars.spec.bs` and `detailTracks.spec.bs`, the helpers both halves call moved to base suites (`tests/helpers/playerCaptions.bs`, `detailScreen.bs`); every test line is unchanged | — |

| AG-314 | One MediaTask at a time (PR 4 review) | Every MediaTask writes `tmp:/subtitles` (numbering from `track1.srt`) and the session cache's `tmp:/sidecar-cache`, and a retiring one still works through its queue: its last requests are the cache's sweep, with the keep list from when its player closed, and `finish`, which clears `tmp:/subtitles`. A replacement started meanwhile, by the next episode (`clearSession`) or a newly opened player, could lose files it had already written and assigned. `stopMediaTask` now records the retiring task in `m.global.retiringMediaTask`, and `sendMedia` starts no MediaTask while that one's `state` is `run`: requests queue, and the player starts its task once the retiring one has ended (`onMediaTaskEnded`); a closed player starts none. Cost: a new player's first subtitle work waits for any conversion the retiring task still had queued, about 4.4 s for the 10.24 MiB ASS of hardware run 26 at most, once per player change. Chosen over per-task directories, which would leave the shared cache's stale sweep. Headless: `tests/player/captionSidecars.spec.bs`, "starts no MediaTask while the one before still deletes its files". Not yet run on the device. |

| AG-316 | Server remux subtitle renditions | (Superseded in part by [D-7](#decisions-maintainer-answers-2026-10-21): G13 is gone, so every build sends `hls_remux_subtitle_renditions_v1` when the server advertises it.) Embedded text subtitles on a copy remux arrive as a WebVTT rendition inside the HLS stream, like a transcode's (section 6.8), when the client sends client feature `hls_remux_subtitle_renditions_v1` and the server advertises it; the Roku sends it under G13 alongside `hls_subtitle_renditions_v1`, which the server requires with it. Before, a remux's embedded track was a sidecar extracted from the whole file: 279 s on a 4K remux, past the 120 s subtitle deadline. On the Roku Ultra (Roku OS 16.0.4) choosing an embedded English SubRip on a DV P7 remux played with `captions="webvtt"` 8 s later, replan and reload included. | AG-324 | Roadmap phase 2: removing a card from a paged list | **Assumed.** The list endpoints' cursors name the item they follow (the contract's fixtures carry keyset cursors such as `{"a":<added_at>,"m":<id>}` and `{"w":<watched_at>,"i":<id>}`), so after the server removes an item a held cursor still reads the item after it. `catalog.grid.removed` therefore moves `hi`, `nextAt`, the total, and every mark after the removed index up one and keeps a mark at that index, and BrowseGrid drops the card without reading the list again. Each change shows at once (the rollup marked through `catalog.personal.markedRollup`, the card's flag set), and once the last write for the change is applied the page reads again only the touched seasons' episodes it holds, the series' seasons (`GET /api/v2/catalog/series/{id}/seasons`, for each season's counts and the series' flag, every season with episodes counting as watched, as tvOS's `refreshWatchedSeason` sets `isWatched`), and the target's watch state, not the whole item, so the hero, the pills, the rail, and focus stay where they are. Any read that lands while a write is out keeps what the page shows for what the write covers: a movie's watched flag in its watch state, an episode card's flag while a mark for the episode, its season, or the series is out, a season's counts while one for the season or the series is out, and the series' flag while one for it is out; a series' seasons are marked from its change only once its last write is applied. Each read again is numbered per thing it reads (a target's watch state, after playback or a mark, a season's episodes, the series' seasons), and an answer older than one already applied for it is dropped, since the reads of a season's episodes and after playback are not cancelled by newer ones. A first read of the series' seasons or of a season's episodes (a pill's, or a neighbour's for the rail) that left before a watched write was applied is read again once it lands (`markedSince`), since the reads after the write cover only what the page held. Code: `source/catalog/translation.bs` (pure), `components/screens/detail/DetailTranslation.bs`, `components/screens/home/HomeTranslation.bs`, `components/views/metadataAI.bs`. |
| AG-601 | Server contract: the capability | `allowed` on this profile-scoped document is the generic capability wrapper's `state == available` (`internal/apiv2/capability_response.go`); there is no per-profile or child-profile switch. The client, as tvOS, treats `available` and `allowed` as enabled and reads `on_view` only then; `on_view` is an open string, so anything but `button` or `auto` reads as `off`. A failed or missing read (404 on an older server) is off. |
| AG-602 | Server contract: what a translation returns | `POST .../translate-description` answers 202 with the job (`MetadataTranslationJob`: status pending, running, completed, failed, canceled); it coalesces onto an active job and may return a job that failed within the cooldown without starting work. There is no viewer job read, so completion is seen by reading the item (or the season's episodes) until `pending_translation_language` goes away; the text lands in the profile's localization tables and every later read carries it, for every profile reading that language. The target must equal the language the profile's read reported (422 at `body.target_language` otherwise, or once nothing is missing). Errors: 409 or 503 when on-view translation is off or not configured, 503 while the profile's preferences are degraded, 429 past the per-account budget (5 a second, 30 a minute, burst 10, `Retry-After`). |
| AG-603 | Read schedule and failure handling | **Assumed from tvOS.** Reads after 1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 4, 5, 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 s (45 s cap, `DescriptionTranslationCoordinator.defaultSchedule`); a failed read counts as still missing. A refused POST is read once, done if the text is there, else failed; `Retry-After` is not honoured, as on tvOS. A failure is silent: the status goes, and More offers Translate Description again (Android TV shows a retry button instead; Roku follows tvOS). The capability is read once per server and profile per session (spec 5.9) and kept in `m.global.metadataAI` under the server and profile key, so a profile or server switch never inherits it. |
| AG-604 | Detail page lifecycle | **Assumed from tvOS's `onDisappear` cancel.** The player over the page (onHide) or Back stops a run and cancels its requests; an automatic run that stopped may start again when the page shows. A failed automatic run stays failed for the page's life, so the page never loops on a failing item. A season run reads `GET /api/v2/catalog/series/{id}/seasons/{n}/episodes` with the page's `library_id` and copies only the overview, AI mark, and pending language onto the page's episode cards; watched state and progress stay the page's. |
| AG-605 | Home hero | **Assumed from tvOS and Android TV.** Only cards of a section with `featured` true translate on Home, only in `auto` mode, after 1.5 s of rest (`TVSkylineSectionFeed.translationDwell`); the item is read without `library_id`, as tvOS reads it for the marquee. The landed text is written onto the card node, so the hero shows it until Home reads its rows again, which then carry it from the server. One run at a time (spec 5.9). Library pages show the AI mark but never start a translation, as tvOS translates only Featured on view. |
| AG-606 | Status line metrics | **Assumed.** tvOS draws the status inside the synopsis block at 21 pt with the translate symbol; Roku draws the words 6 px under the synopsis in the hero synopsis style (22 px Regular) at the credit line's 70%, and the line's height comes out of the synopsis's room so the action row does not move on a series. On Home the status leads the meta line, joined by " · ", as `TVFocusMarquee` leads it. The synopsis dims to 50% while translating, as tvOS's `TVHeroSynopsis`. |
| AG-607 | Metadata Language row | Settings writes `catalog.metadata_language` (profile scope, `language_tag`, nullable; the settings manifest's `catalog_metadata_languages` option set; unset label "Library default"), the key behind `Profile.preferred_metadata_language`. It changes the language the server answers descriptions and taglines in (provider text where it has it, else AI text, else the original with `pending_translation_language`); titles are never translated. The row shows when the capability is enabled, as tvOS's `metadataEnabled` (Android TV also requires `on_view` other than `off`). `x-silo-original` is not offered, as tvOS does not offer it; a value another client set shows with its name. |
| AG-608 | After a metadata language change | **Assumed.** Settings bumps `m.global.metadataRevision` after a successful write, and Home reads its rows again the next time it shows (as for a personal change). Nothing else is cached on Roku; a library page reads its rows when it opens (spec 5.9). |
| AG-609 | Child profiles | Neither tvOS, Android TV, nor the server treats child profiles differently: the capability is per profile but has no child switch, and translation needs only access to the item (`EnsureAccessible`). Roku adds no policy; a profile that can see an item can translate its description. |
| AG-610 | Episode spoiler hiding (AG-460, merged first) | The status says nothing for a withheld description (`catalog.translation.status`'s `shown`): the detail hero's `paintTranslation` asks `catalog.spoilers.hidesOverview` for the episode it describes, and Home's hero needs a non-empty synopsis, which `synopsisOf` empties for a spoiler. A Featured card whose description is withheld starts no translation (`heroTranslates`), as tvOS passes no pending language for a hidden synopsis. A season still translates while its rows are withheld; the text shows once the viewer turns the setting off or watches the episode. |

(2) For You and the Calendar get no card: their rows have no section page (Android TV says so; tvOS's `RecommendationsView` passes none). (3) The card is a `CardContent` with `itemType` "more" and ID `silo.row.more`, so focus restoration, the audio guide, and the RowList treat it as a card; `BrowseRows.moreSelected` reports its row instead of `selectedItem`. The Requests branch ends its Discover rows with the same card labelled "See all" and opens the same page (route `row`, params `{ title, request }`, `rowPage.destination` for where a card opens), agreed with that agent on 2026-10-28. (4) A pending row (sent without items) gets its card when its items load, since a row of placeholders has nothing to explore yet. | tvOS or Android TV give featured rows or For You rows a page |
